In "you shall love with all your soul", the soul in a body gives up every foreign want, even life itself; that is commanded (line 248). Then two words for love. "Aveh" is a will hidden in the soul, not yet felt. "Ahavah", with the extra letter, is the same will come out as a felt love in the heart (line 249). "You shall love" joins the two, beginning and end (line 249). And the second self-giving has the advantage, "for He consulted only with the souls of the righteous in bodies, not with souls as they are in themselves" (line 249). R. Akiva longed all his life, "when will it come to my hand, that I may fulfill it?" (line 249). Bring it home. It may feel more spiritual to be moved in prayer than to give up a craving at lunch. The text says the second is what He wanted most. The picture of R. Akiva breaks for most of us: we are not asked for martyrdom. We are asked for the small daily giving-up that is the same thing in miniature.

**From inside.** A new respect for small self-denials. They stop feeling petty and start feeling like the main event. Some feel a quiet strength.

**At the edge.** It points there: the soul in a body is what "rose before the beginning of thought" (line 247). The mind can make the small sacrifice; what it reaches is above the mind.

**Into the day.** Give up one small want today, on purpose, and say: "With all my soul."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §42: the King's inner palace; the delight that never stops (lines 250-253)

**The movement.** It takes the mind through a king's many halls, where he eats, judges, walks, to the one inner room where he lives and studies. The Torah is that room. And the delight there is constant, like a father's in his son.

> לפי שאין עצמות אא"ס שורה אלא בחכמה לפי ששעשועי' העצמיי' שלו בקביעות תמידי אינו אלא בחכמ' שבתורה דוקא
> *For the Essence of the Infinite dwells only in wisdom, since His essential delight, fixed and constant, is only in the wisdom of the Torah.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: why does "love" lead straight to "these words shall be on your heart", Torah study always, at home and on the way (line 250)? And how can Israel be called His "brothers and friends", when "none is holy like God" (lines 250-251)? Israel and Torah were chosen together in His will before anything (line 251). Step by step, the picture: a king's palace. There is a hall for meals, a hall for judgment, rooms for servants and officers. He goes to each at set times. There are hidden halls he strolls through alone at night. But there is one inner room where no one else enters, where he lives day and night and thinks and studies (lines 251-252). "The Holy One rides at night on His light cherub and roams eighteen thousand worlds", but only in passing (line 252). His fixed home is wisdom: "the Torah is the Holy One's upper palace" (line 252). Why wisdom? Because a person is called by his thinking self, "a speaking soul", and a child is born "in his image" from the father's mind (line 253). And the delight in what is essential never stops: a father's joy in his son, the love of Jonathan and David, is constant, while other pleasures must come and go to stay pleasures (line 253). Bring it home. When you open a book of Torah, you are walking into the room where He lives, not a hall He visits. The palace picture breaks: a king's inner room keeps others out. This one lets you in.

**From inside.** Warmth and privacy, like being invited into the kitchen of a great house. Study feels less like work and more like company.

**At the edge.** It points to "His essential delights", "in His very Essence" (lines 252-253). The mind learns the words; the delight that fills the room is His.

**Into the day.** Set one fixed time today, even five minutes, to sit in that room with a page of Torah.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §43: brothers and friends in one palace (lines 254-257)

**The movement.** It turns the Torah from a garment into a house that surrounds both the learner and God. When a person studies, God studies the same words across from him, inside the same walls. Two become companions, and a third knot ties them.

> כך הקב"ה מוקף באותו היכל עצמו שהאדם מוקף בה
> *So the Holy One is surrounded by the very same palace that surrounds the person.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: a garment fits the body part by part. A house is different: it surrounds a person above, below and on all sides, and "the glory of a man is to sit in his house" (line 254). That is the deepest surrounding of the soul, its "four cubits", which never shines openly but is always around it (line 255). The four cubits of halacha are that for God: His own place (line 255). Step by step: the letters of the Torah are the stones. The cantillation marks above are the roof; the letters are the floor; the sides are its reasons for and against, its length from heaven, its breadth "wider than the sea" (line 255). Whoever reads the letters is surrounded by them, "his whole mind and heart settled inside" (line 256). Now the picture: two people sitting in one palace, reading one halacha, one across from the other. "When one sits and learns, the Holy One sits and learns opposite him." So "the Holy One is surrounded by the very same palace that surrounds the person" (line 256). That is why Israel is called "My brothers and friends" (line 256). And because "none is holy like God", He comes down to delight in this (line 256). "The souls came down to receive the Torah so that He would have a dwelling below in the four cubits of halacha, through them" (line 257). Bring it home. Read one line of Torah aloud today, and imagine the voice across the table reading it with you. The picture breaks where it must: two friends are equals. Here One is beyond all, and still sits in the same room.

**From inside.** Companionship. Loneliness in learning softens. A quiet joy of being studied with, not only studying.

**At the edge.** It points there: the house is "a surrounding of the surrounding", rooted in "the unbounded circle in the Essence" (line 256). The mind sits in the room; the One across the table is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Learn one halacha today with someone, or aloud alone, and say: "We are learning this together."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §44: love from far, awe from near; God is called Peace (lines 258-263)

**The movement.** It notices that love grows with distance and awe with nearness, two opposite motions in one heart. Then it names the only thing that can make them one: the Essence Himself, who is therefore called "Peace". It ends with joy and trembling in the very same place.

> שזה א"א כ"א מצד בחי' העצמו' שלמעלה מהכל ע"כ נק' שלום
> *This is possible only from the Essence, which is above everything; therefore He is called Peace.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: love and awe are opposites (line 258). A father's love for his son burns most when the son is far away; "Jacob's spirit revived" at the news of Joseph (line 258). Israel's love in exile is "flames of fire", more than in the Temple; "my soul thirsts for You in a dry and weary land" (lines 258-259). Even the highest worlds are "desert" compared to Him, so thirst is everywhere (line 259). Awe is the reverse: it comes from nearness, from realizing He is here, "no place empty of Him" (line 259). Step by step: how can one command, "you shall love the (et) Lord", hold both? "Peace, peace, to the far and to the near": the far becomes near (line 259). That joining is not ordinary; it is like "He makes peace in His heights" between fire and water. And "this is possible only from the Essence, above everything; therefore He is called Peace" (line 260). Then higher: "Because you did not serve with joy and a good heart." In the mitzvot, joy and trembling are not only side by side, joy inside and awe outside; they are in one place, "where there is rejoicing, there let there be trembling" (lines 261-262). That too comes only from the Essence, revealed through the mitzvot. And speech in Torah, "with awe", carries it; the tefillin are the witness to it all (lines 262-263). Bring it home. Think of someone you love who is far away: feel the longing. Then of standing before someone great, close up: feel the hush. Now think of Him, both far and here, and let both feelings stand. The picture of the father and son breaks: they are two. Here the joining is by the One who is above both far and near.

**From inside.** A settled heart that holds opposites without strain. A joy that has seriousness in it, a seriousness that does not kill joy.

**At the edge.** Yes. Only "the Essence above everything" joins far and near; that is why He is Peace (line 260). The mind can hold one feeling at a time; peace comes from beyond it.

**Into the day.** At the next mitzvah you do today, let yourself feel glad and serious at once, and say "Shalom".

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §45: all from nothing; "in the place of His greatness, His humility" (lines 264-266)

**The movement.** It levels the whole ladder of worlds: from the highest to the lowest, everything comes from Him across the same gap, "something from nothing". Then it shows the humility of the Infinite: the greater He is, the lower He can bend.

> והיינו כמו שארז"ל במקו' גדולתו שם אתה מוצא ענותנותו
> *As the Sages said: in the place of His greatness, there you find His humility.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the two paragraphs of the Shema, love and the yoke, exchange kindness and severity, each light in the other's vessel (lines 264-265). The text counts the words, forty-two and seventy-two, and says the two ways of counting are "truly one" (line 265). Step by step, the great rule: all of the chain is "something from nothing", even the highest world, Atzilut, before its Emanator (line 265). There is no comparison between a limited creation and its unlimited Creator, and just as little between Atzilut and the One above it. So "from the side of the source, all is one, coming from the Essence of the Infinite across the leap of being" (line 265). "I am first and I am last; see, I, I am He" (line 265). How did anything come out, if He is not any quality at all? He could have given without end; He limited Himself to ten (line 266). "In the place of His greatness, there you find His humility": the power to limit is in His greatness, and the humility is "in proportion to the height of the Essence" (line 266). The higher one is, the lower one can bend (line 266). At first He did this out of His own wish for kindness; afterward it waits for the lower ones to stir it (line 266). Bring it home. Think of the greatest person you have met who was also the humblest, who could sit with a child. The text says that is a small picture of Him. The picture breaks: a humble great person is still limited. His humility is without limit, like His greatness.

**From inside.** Tenderness and awe together. The sense that God's coming down to you is not a condescension but His greatness itself. Some feel less small.

**At the edge.** Yes. From the source "all is one"; Atzilut and our world are equal before Him (line 265). The mind sees the ladder; the One at its source sees no rungs.

**Into the day.** Do one humble thing today for someone smaller than you, and think of whose greatness it copies.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §46: "Shema Yisrael": what a soul in a body lifts (lines 267-270)

**The movement.** It says that the first two words of the Shema are a lifting, and that only a soul still in a body can do it. What it lifts is the stuff of ordinary life: food, clothing, business, feelings. The mind is turned to see its daily life as the material of the Shema.

> וכן כל מה שאכל ולבש ועסק במו"מ וכל עניני עוה"ז שכולן מקליפות נוגה הכל עולה לשרשם
> *And everything he ate and wore and did in business, all the matters of this world, which all come from the mixed shell, all rises to its root.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the six words of the first verse are the higher unity, and they are made by souls. "Shema Yisrael" is the raising; "Havayah Elokeinu", a new light drawn into wisdom and understanding in response (lines 267-268). Even "the lowest soul in Israel" can say "our God" (line 268). Step by step: a soul without a body can cling above, but that is not the lifting the Shema speaks of. "This is not the raising, except when the soul is still in a body" (line 269). What is lifted are the fallen sparks: the animal soul's natural feelings, and all the pleasures of this world, which come from a mixed realm, neither holy nor unholy (line 269). When a person thinks about God with his human mind and is moved in his flesh heart, those very feelings rise "from separation to unity" (line 270). "And everything he ate and wore and did in business, all the matters of this world, all rises to its root" (line 270). The divine soul "needs no repair"; it came down only to refine these garments (line 270). The picture: God's presence going down into exile to gather, "she rises while it is still night, and gives food to her household" (line 269). Bring it home. Look at your plate, your clothes, your work today. None of it is outside the Shema. It is what the Shema lifts, through you. The picture of lifting breaks: you do not carry things up a ladder. They rise when you turn your heart toward Him while using them.

**From inside.** The day stops being divided into holy and ordinary. A sense of carrying your life with you into prayer rather than leaving it at the door.

**At the edge.** It points to the root of these sparks, "above the name of Adam", in the hidden levels before the world's repair (line 270). The mind does the lifting; where it lifts to is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Before the Shema tomorrow, think of one thing you ate or did yesterday, and bring it with you into "Shema Yisrael".

## Shaar Kriat Shema §47: "David made a name"; before Him, dark and light are the same (lines 271-274)

**The movement.** It first raises the dwelling below to the reason for everything. Then, from His side, it lowers it: before His Essence, broken and mended, hidden and revealed, are the same. The mind is steadied by being told its drama is real and yet does not change Him.

> שכל השנוי הזה בין העלם וגלוי לא שייך רק לגבי המקבלים למטה ולא לגבי עצמותו
> *All this change between hidden and revealed belongs only to the receivers below, not to His Essence.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "David made a name": the refined sparks become a crown, "a good name" (line 271). This lifting "rose first in thought"; it is the hidden pleasure in His Essence that there be a breaking, a sorting and a repair, "the Holy One desired a dwelling below" (line 271). Step by step, then the other side. "I am first and I am last": before the breaking and after the repair, "all is equal before His very Essence", "darkness and light alike" (line 273). The picture: sunlight and its eclipse change nothing in the sun itself (line 273). So "all this change between hidden and revealed belongs only to the receivers below, not to His Essence" (line 273). Even the pleasure in the refinements "counts as nothing" beside the simple delight in His Essence (line 273). And still the lifting matters: when "Shema Yisrael" raises the sparks, a new light comes down into wisdom and understanding; otherwise, the text says, nothing new would be drawn (lines 273-274). Bring it home. Your struggles are real, and your successes count. And under them, He is not tossed about by them. Both are true. Let the second hold the first. The picture of the sun breaks: the sun does not care about the earth. He does; He "consulted the souls", and still is not changed.

**From inside.** Steadiness. Less panic about spiritual ups and downs. A humility that is also relief: the Infinite is not fragile.

**At the edge.** Yes. His "simple delight in His very Essence", beside which the refinements count as nothing (line 273). The mind lives in the drama; it can know there is a place above it.

**Into the day.** On a bad day, say once: "Dark and light are the same before You," and then do the next small good thing anyway.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §48: "One": opposites made one before their teacher (lines 275-277)

**The movement.** It gives the mind a picture it can see: two schools who disagree, forbidding and permitting, who become one when they stand before their teacher. So God's opposite qualities are one before their source. And it adds the humble wisdom of being a tail to lions.

> וה"ז כהתכללות ב' הפכי' לגבי העליון הכוללם יחד
> *This is like the inclusion of two opposites before the higher one who holds them together.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "echad" means things that are divided becoming one. Of what has no second, one says not "unity" but "yachid" (line 275). "Havayah echad" is God's qualities, kindness and severity, truly different, made one (line 275). Step by step, the picture: two groups who learned together, then split, Shammai forbidding and Hillel permitting. When they come before their teacher, they are one, because the source of both their understandings is one, undivided (line 275). So above: kindness and severity are one before the Infinite light that holds them, "like the inclusion of two opposites before the higher one who holds them together" (line 275). Even in a person, one feels the life in the head and in the toe as one life (line 275). Another saying: "Be a tail to lions, not a head to foxes." A disciple nullified before a great teacher, standing close behind him, is higher than one who leaves to lead lesser people (line 276). The text uses it for the kingship of God that first shrinks to "a single point" beside the giver, then is built up. Then "Baruch Shem" is the lower unity, with time in it: "He reigned, He reigns, He will reign." The higher unity is above time. In the future, time itself will be eternal, "because what is above time will shine in time" (line 277). Bring it home. Think of two good people you know who disagree. Picture them before someone they both revere. Then think of two opposite pulls in your own heart, before Him.

**From inside.** A softening of inner conflict. Opposites in you stop feeling like a war. Some feel the dignity of being a small disciple of the great.

**At the edge.** It points there: the source of both sides has "no division at all", the Infinite in His Essence, "not of these qualities at all" (line 275). The mind stands between the opposites; the One who holds them is above it.

**Into the day.** When you disagree with someone today, think of the One before whom you both stand, and speak from there.

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §49: "with all your might"; fire inside the water (lines 278-281)

**The movement.** It shows two layers in love. On the outside, love flows like water, warm and clinging. Inside, hidden, is a fire that wants to leave every vessel and be swallowed in Him. The mind learns that the Shema's inner intention lives in that hidden fire.

> ותוך פנימי' הלב הוא רשפי אש התשוקה בנקוד' לבו לצאת מכלי הגבלתו
> *And inside, within the heart, are the flames of the fire of longing, in the point of his heart, to leave its limiting vessel.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the first paragraph says "with all your might", without limit; the second says only "with all your heart and soul" (line 278). "All your might" is giving up the body's own will entirely, "my flesh and my heart long", to go out of the body's darkness, out of the vessel altogether (line 279). Step by step, the picture: a person's love for a friend or a spouse. On the outside it flows like water: clinging, drawing close, spreading. Inside it is fire: a longing that wants to give up everything, "like the love of David and Jonathan", when Jonathan stripped off his cloak for his friend (line 280). The outer love is the vessel; the inner fire is the light. So "you shall love" is the outer, flowing love, which takes work, "the service of love" (line 280). And inside it, not written in the words at all, only hidden in the heart's intention, is the running-out, "all your might" (line 280). "If your heart runs, return" (line 280). This, says the text, is accepting "the yoke of heaven": giving up one's will entirely (line 280). Bring it home. When you say "you shall love" today, say it plainly, with the warmth you have. And know that under the warmth there may be a fire you cannot feel, that wants only Him. The picture breaks: in human love, the fire can burn out. This fire, the text says, is the soul's own nature toward its source.

**From inside.** A double awareness: a steady, usable love, and beneath it a hidden intensity that does not need to be felt to be real. Some notice a quiet ache.

**At the edge.** Yes. The fire wants "to be included in the Essence of Godliness", beyond every vessel (line 279). The mind cannot follow it out; it can only say the words and let the fire be.

**Into the day.** Say "with all your might" slowly in today's Shema, and mean more than you can feel.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §50: the return is the main thing (lines 282-285)

**The movement.** It turns the whole spiritual climb upside down. The rising toward God is not the goal; it is for the sake of coming back down into the world, into the mitzvah, into a vessel. The mind that wanted to escape is sent home with a purpose.

> והשוב הוא העיקר וכל ההעלא' דרצוא לא הי' רק בשביל הירידה דשוב
> *The return is the main thing; all the rising of the running was only for the sake of coming back down.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the first paragraph is running, the yoke of heaven; the second is returning, the yoke of mitzvot, light settling in vessels (line 282). "Why does Shema come before Vehayah? To accept the yoke of heaven first, and then the yoke of mitzvot" (line 283). Step by step: usually a descent is for the sake of an ascent: a rest in a journey is for traveling on, a bird pulls its wing back to fly forward (line 283). Here it is the reverse: "the return is the main thing; all the rising of the running was only for the sake of coming back down" (line 283). A king's rule is not complete when he is crowned; it is complete when his commands are done (line 283). "He did not create it for chaos; He formed it to be dwelt in" (line 283). The soul that longs to be swallowed up in Him "turns and longs" for His light to be revealed in its study and deeds, in "vessels of many kinds" (line 284). And the depth of the running sets the depth of the return: "each hangs on the other, in exact proportion" (line 284). The rising purifies; the coming down brings the Higher down whole (line 284). Bring it home. After a moment of real closeness in prayer, notice the pull to stay there. The text says: good, and now go down. Take it into your next conversation, your next task. The picture of the bird breaks here on purpose: the text uses it to show the opposite. This flight is for landing.

**From inside.** A settling back into the body and the day, without loss. The high moment is not over; it is being delivered. A quiet readiness.

**At the edge.** It touches it: the running seeks to be "included in the Essence", and the Essence's own will is the return (line 283). The mind cannot hold both; it can do the return.

**Into the day.** After prayer today, walk straight into one mitzvah with the feeling from prayer still in you.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §51: the bridge, the raindrops, and kindness that must be divided (lines 286-289)

**The movement.** It shows that holding back is part of giving. A great kindness must be divided into small portions or it would drown the one it is meant for. The mind learns to see the measure in its blessings as love, not as stinginess.

> ואמנם אינו בחי' דין וצמצום אמיתי אדרבה זהו עיקר החסד
> *Yet this is not true judgment and restraint; on the contrary, this is the essence of kindness.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: kindness by nature flows down like water. If His kindness came down as it is, it would be a flood, and "would not be received at all" (line 286). Step by step, the picture: a bridge over a great river. Its base is built of many small chambers, so the water passes through each one a little at a time. Without them the river would sweep the bridge away; the bigger the river, the more chambers are needed (lines 284-286). A great teacher does the same: his wisdom is an ocean, so he divides it into many small introductions and parables, each carrying a little, until the student can hold the whole (line 286). So the dividing that looks like strictness "is not true judgment; on the contrary, it is the essence of kindness" (line 287). Rain comes in drops; if it came all at once it would be the Flood (line 287). Noah fed each animal its own food; the wrong food, however generous, would kill (line 288). And all the many kinds of kindness, light, water, whiteness, sweetness, the kind man, the merciful eagle, "surely have one source" (line 288). Bring it home. Think of a blessing that came to you slowly, in pieces, when you wanted it all at once. The text says the slowness was itself the kindness, fitted to you. The picture of the bridge breaks: engineers build against a river. He divides His own kindness out of love for the receiver.

**From inside.** Patience that feels like trust. Less resentment about "not yet". Gratitude for small portions.

**At the edge.** It points there: the kindness in its source is "in the manner of the Infinite", too great to be received at all (line 286). The mind receives the drops; the source is beyond it.

**Into the day.** When something comes to you in a small portion today, say: "This is Your kindness, measured for me."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §52: "I will give the rain of your land"; a mouth that is only a vessel (lines 290-293)

**The movement.** It notices that in the second paragraph Moses speaks as if he himself gives the rain. The answer is bitul: a person so empty of self that God speaks through him. And after "with all your might", anyone can become a small vessel like that.

> רוח ה' מדבר בו ואין דבורו רק כלי לדבר הוי'
> *The spirit of God speaks in him, and his speech is only a vessel for the word of God.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: Moses spoke the Shema to Israel, and yet in the second paragraph he says "I will give the rain of your land", in God's first person (line 290). And rain fits this paragraph: it comes in drops, kindness measured for the receiver (line 290). Step by step: the Infinite clothes itself only in wisdom, which is bitul, "what am I?", one who does not feel himself at all (line 290). Like Rav Ulla, who learned constantly "and did not credit himself". To such a person God gives the higher wisdom, and "the spirit of God speaks in him, and his speech is only a vessel for the word of God" (line 290). The Maggid told R. Yosef Karo: "I am the Mishnah speaking in your mouth" (line 291). But one cannot reach this without first "with all your might", running out of the self (line 290). And the text says: after the first paragraph's self-giving, "any person can say 'I will give'" in this paragraph, as a vessel for the Infinite in his soul (line 291). "In proportion to the longing of the running, so is the bitul that makes one a vessel" (line 291). Bring it home. When you study or speak Torah today, try for a moment to get out of the way. Not "I am explaining this", but "these words are passing through me". The picture of Moses breaks for us: we are not prophets. The text still insists that each person can become, in measure, a vessel.

**From inside.** A lightness in speaking. Less performance, more transparency. Some feel a surprising clarity when the self steps back.

**At the edge.** It points there: the Infinite rests "only in the bitul of wisdom" (line 291). The mind cannot make itself nothing; it can stop insisting on itself.

**Into the day.** Before teaching or sharing any Torah today, say quietly: "Let me be only a vessel."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §53: bread of Torah; a child learns to say "Father" (lines 294-296)

**The movement.** It gives Torah study a homely picture: bread, chewed well. A child first says "Abba" after tasting grain. So the soul knows its Father in heaven by tasting the reasons of the Talmud. The mind is invited to chew slowly.

> וכך בנשמה האלקית אין בה בחי' הדעת להכיר לאביו שבשמים עד שיטעום דגן שהוא התלמוד דוקא
> *So too the divine soul has no knowledge to recognize its Father in heaven until it tastes grain, which is the Talmud.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "You will gather your grain, your wine and your oil" (line 294). Grain is the revealed Torah, bread for the soul; wine and oil are its inner secrets (lines 294-295). "A child does not know how to say 'Father' and 'Mother' until he tastes the taste of grain" (line 294). Step by step: the text raises its own objection. Enoch knew God before there was a Talmud (line 294). The answer: the ancients knew Him as "God Almighty", as King; they did not know Him "as a son knows a father". The Torah is rooted in the essence of His mind, so tasting it gives a son's knowing (line 295). The picture: bread is only tasty when baked, not as raw wheat. And its taste comes when the teeth grind it and the palate tastes it, before it even reaches the stomach (lines 294-295). So "clarifying the halacha" is like grinding with the teeth: breaking the law into its parts, tasting its reason. The Mishnah states the ruling; the Talmud gives its reason, the taste (line 294). "Not by bread alone does a person live, but by all that comes from God's mouth" in the bread (line 295). Bring it home. When you learn a halacha, do not swallow it whole. Ask "why?" and chew until you taste the reason. That tasting, says the text, is where the soul says "Abba". The bread picture breaks: bread fills you and is gone. This bread leaves you knowing whose child you are.

**From inside.** A warmth in study, like being fed at home. Some feel for the first time that learning is intimate, not only intellectual.

**At the edge.** It points to "wine and oil", the secrets above the reasons (line 295). The mind tastes the bread; the inner wine is further in.

**Into the day.** Learn one halacha today and ask "why?" three times until you taste its reason.

**Ladder.** L07 (Know it today, bring it to the heart)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §54: wine that reveals, oil that stays hidden; the secret of joy (lines 297-299)

**The movement.** It contrasts two liquids as two kinds of hidden wisdom. Oil floats above everything and seeps into everything, yet never mixes. Wine was hidden a long time so it could reveal. And joy, too, brings out what is deepest. The mind learns to love both the hidden and the revealed.

> ששרש השמחה מגיע בעצם רוממות הנפש בתענוג הפשוט שכל סוד שם יגלה
> *For the root of joy reaches the very height of the soul, the simple delight where every secret comes out.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: oil floats on wine, water and milk and never truly mixes; shake it and it rises back to the top. Yet oil seeps deeper into food and cloth than any other liquid (line 297). That is wisdom: set apart, and still in everything, "even in stone" (line 297). The text uses this to explain secrets and "secrets of secrets" (lines 297-298). Step by step, wine: "wine goes in, a secret comes out" (line 298). Why can wine force a secret out? Because wine itself was hidden a long time in the grapes, growing stronger in hiding, and its whole hiding was for the sake of being revealed (lines 298-299). Oil is the opposite: it stays in silence (line 299). And joy does the same as wine, from a deeper place. In joy, a person cannot hide anything; "the root of joy reaches the very height of the soul, the simple delight where every secret comes out" (line 299). On the altar, wine was poured and the Levites sang, "for there is no song except over wine" (line 299). Wine brings joy; joy is the revealing of the soul's essence (line 299). Bring it home, carefully: the text's wine is a picture, not a practice. The joy it speaks of comes from the soul, through song and mitzvah. Sing one niggun today with your whole self and notice what it brings out. The picture of wine breaks in the obvious way: wine loosens tongues without choosing what comes out. Holy joy reveals the soul's best.

**From inside.** Openness. A readiness to be seen. Some feel a part of themselves that was hidden a long time come quietly into the light.

**At the edge.** It points to "the simple delight" at the soul's height, and to oil, "hidden, never revealed" (line 299). The mind can rejoice; the deepest secret stays with Him.

**Into the day.** Sing one song of praise today, out loud if you can, and let it bring out something true.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §55: David's dance; a secret that stays sealed (lines 300-303)

**The movement.** It sets two pictures side by side: David dancing with everything shown, and a secret so deep it will never be told. It teaches the mind both to rejoice openly and to leave alone what is meant to stay hidden, "for His glory is to be hidden".

> שזהו עיקר כבודו להיות בהעל' דווקא
> *For this is His main glory: to be hidden.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: wine is "the joy of God", understanding that is felt: the delight of grasping His greatness (line 300). So David danced "with all his might", everything he was revealed, until Michal despised him (line 300). R. Akiva's bowing was different: also a revealing, but hidden inside bitul; he did not know himself what his body did (lines 300-301). Step by step, two kinds of secret. One comes out: hidden wisdom that becomes teaching, "wonder" turned into "aleph" (line 301). The other never comes out, not even in the future: "no eye has seen" (line 301). Like the red heifer, which even Solomon said "was far from me" (line 301). Between them is what can be grasped only by hint, "one who understands on his own", given only in chapter headings (line 302). And a warning: "Do not seek what is too wondrous for you", not even for yourself (line 302). Seeking what cannot be revealed "cuts and damages the hidden light", "for this is His main glory: to be hidden" (line 302). Some secrets are His glory to reveal to those who fear Him; others are His glory to keep (line 302). Bring it home. There is a kind of spiritual curiosity that grabs. The text asks for another kind: to rejoice fully in what is given, and to bow before what is not. The picture of David breaks for most of us: we rarely dance like that. And it is not asked of us to force it; only not to be ashamed when joy comes.

**From inside.** Freedom and reverence together. The pressure to understand everything lifts. A respect for mystery that feels like love, not frustration.

**At the edge.** Yes. The secret of secrets "will not be revealed even in the future", and of His Essence "one cannot even speak of a secret revealed" (lines 301-302). The mind comes to a closed door and stands before it gladly.

**Into the day.** Once today, when you do not understand something in Torah, say: "Your glory is to be hidden," and go on learning what you can.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §56: oil: separate from all, drawn into all (lines 304-306)

**The movement.** It gives the mind a picture close to the whole teaching of Chassidus: oil, which never mixes with anything and yet seeps into everything, because it moves hidden. The mind learns that what is most set apart can be most present.

> וזהו שהשמן נבדל מהכל ונמשך בכל לפי שהמשכתו בהעלם
> *This is why oil is separate from everything and drawn into everything: because its drawing is hidden.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the Sages disagree whether oil floating on wine is joined to it for purity law (line 304). The text says both are right: it does not mix, yet it touches and clings (line 304). Oil anoints the High Priest, the vessels of the Temple, the kings; holiness comes through it (line 305). "Their rivers I will make flow like oil" (line 305). It seeps even into flint: "oil from the flinty rock" (line 305). Step by step: how can oil be both hidden and flowing everywhere? Its flowing is not like speech or motion. It is a hidden flowing, "a still, small voice", "the King really comes, but in silence" (line 305). "This is why oil is separate from everything and drawn into everything: because its drawing is hidden" (line 306). So bowing, whispered prayer, a still voice: all flow from bitul, hidden (line 306). That is wisdom's inner part: it never becomes "revealed", yet it is in all things, down to the stone (lines 305-306). Its outer part touches understanding and can even be touched by impurity, but its inner part, "they die, but not with wisdom", is untouched (line 306). Bring it home. Think of how oil works in your kitchen: a few drops, and it is in everything, never seen apart. Let that picture Him in your day: nowhere visible, in everything. The picture breaks: oil is still a substance with a place. What it points to has no place and still is in every place.

**From inside.** A quiet, pervasive presence. Things feel lightly anointed. Some find a peace in not needing to see Him in order to know He is in it.

**At the edge.** Yes. The inner wisdom "will never be revealed" and yet is drawn into everything (line 306). The mind can taste the oil in the food; it cannot separate it out to look at.

**Into the day.** When you cook or eat today, notice the oil in one dish and say: "Separate from all, and in all."

**Ladder.** L04 (Held from inside and all around)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §57: the lamp of the mitzvah; days when learning flows (lines 307-310)

**The movement.** It shows that the light of Torah comes out through the lamp of a mitzvah, oil drawn up a wick. Then it explains why some days the mind understands and some days it does not: the hidden channel above gives more or less. The mind learns to receive its good days as gifts.

> שע"י נר מצוה דוקא בא האור דתורה מהעלם לגלוי
> *It is precisely through the lamp of the mitzvah that the light of Torah comes from hiding into revelation.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "A mitzvah is a lamp and Torah is light": the light of Torah comes out "precisely through the lamp of the mitzvah" (line 308). In a lamp the oil is drawn up the wick and becomes flame. So the hidden Torah becomes seen, "like the shining of a face": "a man's wisdom lights up his face", the revealing of the very self (line 308). Step by step: rain begins as thin threads from a hidden source and only below becomes heavy drops (line 308). Hair grows from a tiny hidden root in the scalp; if two grew from one root, the mind would be confused; so each raindrop has its own source (lines 308-309). Wisdom too comes to a person like something found on the road, from no known place, a flash (line 309). That is why "mazal makes wise": some days a person learns well, finding deep new ideas; some days he does not, and it depends on what the hidden channel receives from above (line 309). "He gives wisdom to the wise", as a gift, "I will give the rain", freely, "because He desires kindness" (lines 309-310). Bring it home. On a day your learning flows, say thank you; it was given. On a day it does not, light a small lamp anyway: do a mitzvah, and let the light come when it comes. The picture of the lamp breaks: a lamp's oil runs out. The oil here is drawn from a source that does not.

**From inside.** Gratitude without pride on good days, patience without shame on dry ones. The face softens.

**At the edge.** It points to "the first source" from which the hidden channels flow, "rich kindness in the Essence" (line 310). The mind receives the rain; the Giver's knowing is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Do one mitzvah today before you study, and think of it as lighting the lamp.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §58: the early rain and the late rain; the nothing of one who sees the King (lines 311-313)

**The movement.** It gives two rains that do opposite work: one helps the seed rot and vanish, the other fills the grain. Then it names two kinds of "nothing", and gives the plainest picture of bitul: what happens to a person who sees the king.

> עד"מ שנמשך ברואה את המלך ביטול היש לגמרי
> *As, for example, in one who sees the king, a complete nullification of self is drawn.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the early rain falls at sowing time, only to moisten the soil so the seed rots quickly and fully, its body gone, its life absorbed into the soil's power to grow (line 311). The late rain falls in spring, when the stalks have grown; it does not make more grains, it fills each grain, fat and full (lines 311-312). One turns something into nothing; the other brings nothing into something (line 312). Step by step: these are the two hidden channels above: one to wisdom, one to understanding (line 312). And wisdom is called "nothing", "koach mah", "what?" (line 313). But what does "nothing" mean here? There are two: the essential nothing of the crown, and the nothing that makes things nothing (line 313). The picture: "one who sees the king". Standing before him, a person's whole self goes quiet, not by effort, but by seeing (line 313). "We are what?", said Moses. And there are degrees: each person is nullified in his own measure (line 313). Bring it home. Remember a moment you stood before something much greater than you: a mountain, a great person, a birth. Your self went quiet by itself. That is the text's picture of the bitul that wisdom brings. The rain picture holds a long way; it breaks where the text says, at the top: the essential nothing is not a nothing that makes anything; it simply is.

**From inside.** A seeing-based quiet, not a forced one. The self steps back because something is in front of it. Some feel a clean, bright humility.

**At the edge.** It points to "the essential nothing in the inner crown", above even the nothing that nullifies (line 313). The mind can be quieted by seeing; the Nothing that is beyond is beyond seeing.

**Into the day.** Before the Amidah today, picture yourself standing before the King, and let yourself go quiet.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §59: insight and grasp; the one who understands one thing from another (lines 314-317)

**The movement.** It takes the mind through its own two talents, the flash of a new idea and the patient grasp that fills it out, and notes that people have one without the other. Then it names a third thing, born when the two exchange roles: the "understanding" person who draws the new from the known.

> ויוצא מזה דבר שלמעלה משניהם והוא הנבון שמבין דבר חדש מתוך דבר
> *And from this comes something higher than both: the discerning one who understands a new thing from within a thing.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the late rain fills out the grain; so understanding (binah) fills out a flash of wisdom into a full grasp, "length and breadth", felt and enjoyed (lines 314-315). A thing understood well gives real delight and life; one not grasped gives none (line 315). Step by step, the text's observation: some people are brilliant at new ideas and cannot grasp an idea fully; others grasp deeply everything they learn and cannot invent anything new (line 315). Each faculty has its own source (lines 315-316). Even the leaning of an idea toward leniency or strictness does not force the grasp to agree: the school of Shammai could accept Hillel's reasoning and still hold to its own (line 316). But when the two include each other, wisdom doing understanding's work and understanding doing wisdom's, "from this comes something higher than both: the discerning one, who understands a new thing from within a thing" (line 317). "In Moses' days they found wise men, but not discerning ones" (line 316). And at Sinai "they saw what is heard, and heard what is seen": each faculty doing the other's work (line 318). Bring it home. Notice which you are more: the one with sparks, or the one who holds. Do not despise your weaker side. Let each lend to the other, and see what new thing comes. The picture of the two rains breaks: rains cannot trade work. Minds can, and that is the gift.

**From inside.** Self-knowledge without judgment. A respect for other kinds of minds. Some feel a new patience with their own way of learning.

**At the edge.** It points to "the source that holds both" above (line 317). The mind can join its two talents; the root that joins them is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Take one idea you learned this week and ask: "What new thing follows from this?"

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §60: rain "in its season"; Torah out of the cloud (lines 318-321)

**The movement.** It resolves two opposite rains into one gift given at two times, and then looks up at Sinai: the light of Torah came out of thick cloud and darkness. The mind learns to expect the brightest light from the densest hiding.

> ומשם דוקא בא אור דתורה לפי ששרש אור דתורה מעצמות החכמ' שבהעלם עצמותו ממש
> *And from there precisely came the light of Torah, because its root is in the essence of wisdom hidden in His very self.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "I will give the rain of your land in its season, early and late" (line 318). How can two opposite rains, months apart, be "in its season", singular? Because "its season" is one root that holds both, and only afterward they divide: the early to let the seed become nothing, the late to fill the grain (line 319). "Each hangs on the other", and "both are one" (line 319). Step by step: rain forms from vapors rising from the earth into clouds. Sometimes the clouds hold the water a long time before it falls; sometimes it does not fall at all (lines 319-320). Above, the waters of wisdom are held in "the darkness of waters, thick clouds", "He made darkness His hiding place", and then break out as rain (line 320). So at Sinai: "thick cloud, darkness and fog", and "from there precisely came the light of Torah", because its root is in the hidden essence of His wisdom (line 320). "Like the advantage of light out of darkness" (line 320). And it came as a gift, "I will give", from His great kindness; it is revealed through the lamp of the mitzvah (line 320). Bring it home. When your understanding of God is clouded, the text does not call that the end of light. It calls it the cloud that light comes out of. The picture breaks: clouds sometimes pass with no rain. The text holds that His hiding is always for the sake of giving, even when the timing is His.

**From inside.** A patient hope in confusion. The cloud looks less like a wall and more like a womb. Some feel ready to wait.

**At the edge.** Yes. The root is "the essence of wisdom hidden in His very self", "depth of depth" (line 320). The mind stands in the cloud; the light's source is beyond it.

**Into the day.** When something in Torah seems dark to you today, say: "Light comes out of this cloud," and keep learning.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §61: grass for your animal first; "I was a beast with You" (lines 322-324)

**The movement.** It says a person must feed his animal before himself, and reads it inward: first the animal soul must be given its share of praise, and only then can the divine soul speak. The mind learns not to leave its plain, natural self behind in prayer.

> וכמ"ש דוד ואני בהמות הייתי עמך בדו"ר טבעיי' דוקא
> *As David said, "I was a beast with You", in natural love and fear.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "I will give grass in your field for your animal, and you shall eat and be satisfied." The Sages learned from this: a person may not eat before he feeds his animal (line 322). Grass is "for the service of man" (line 322). Step by step: there is a great "beast" above that eats the grass of a thousand mountains each day: the sparks sorted from the mixed world rise up every day and are absorbed, like grass that grows back after cutting (line 322). Even in exile, where no light is seen, people return by themselves, "because the Shechinah is with them", like converts rising on their own (line 322). Below, in a person, the animal soul with its natural feelings comes first; the divine soul comes into its own later, at thirteen, when there is real knowing (line 323). So in prayer: Pesukei deZimra and the blessings before the Shema feed the animal soul, giving it its natural love and fear, "praise like the angels". Only then comes "Shema Yisrael", the soul itself (lines 323-324). "I was a beast with You", said David: a beast, but with You (line 324). Bring it home. Your plain, animal self, hungry, tired, wanting, is not to be pushed aside at the door of prayer. Give it the Psalms first. Let it praise in its own simple way. The picture breaks kindly: a farmer feeds his animal because it is his. Here the animal self is also you, and is being lifted, not only fed.

**From inside.** Relief at being allowed to come as you are. A simple, bodily gratitude that opens the way for deeper prayer.

**At the edge.** It does not reach the edge here. It is about the order of prayer, the ground floor before the Shema.

**Into the day.** Say one Psalm of praise slowly before the Shema tomorrow, as food for your simplest self.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §62: the shape of the aleph; shrinking to give, shrinking to receive (lines 325-327)

**The movement.** It looks at the letter aleph, two small yuds joined by a line, and finds a lesson about teaching and learning. The one who gives shrinks himself to a point; the one who receives shrinks himself to a point; the more one shrinks to receive, the more one receives.

> וכל שהוא בא בבחי' הצמצום יותר בעצמו לקבל יקבל הרב' יותר ויוכל להיות אח"כ משפיע ברבוי התפשטו' יותר
> *And the more one contracts oneself in order to receive, the more one receives, and afterward one can give with greater abundance.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the aleph of "echad" is the intermediary between the Essence and wisdom (lines 325-326). Its shape is a yud above, a slanted line, and a yud below. The upper yud is "wonder", hidden; the lower is "teaching", revealed; and the line joins them (line 326). Wonder turned around becomes aleph; "the one who does wonders" (line 327). Step by step, the picture: a great teacher, to teach a small student, gathers his whole mind to one point, "shrinking to give". The student, to receive, must also shrink himself to a point, "shrinking to receive" (line 326). "And the more one contracts oneself in order to receive, the more one receives, and afterward one can give with greater abundance" (line 326). The text adds: as a person grows from seven to seventy, his power of wisdom is not only revealed more; sometimes it truly grows, "He gives wisdom to the wise", a new light in the very power (line 327). Bring it home. When you come to learn or to listen, empty yourself of what you already know for a moment. Make yourself small, one point. The text promises that this makes room for more. The picture breaks at the top: a teacher shrinks because he is limited in words. He shrinks because He chooses to give.

**From inside.** Openness. The mind stops defending and starts receiving. Afterward, a surprising fullness to share.

**At the edge.** It points to "the Essence, above even being a source for wisdom", which the upper yud touches (line 326). The mind can make itself a point; what flows into the point is beyond it.

**Into the day.** The next time someone teaches you something, say inwardly "I know nothing about this yet," and listen.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §63: humility, the sign of true "nothing" (lines 328-331)

**The movement.** It explains why wisdom goes to the humble: humility is the outward sign of a self that has become "what?". Then it turns the same truth upward, to God's own humility, and to the way a new idea hovers over the mind before it lands.

> והענוה סימן לבחי' תכלית הבטול והאין דחכמ'
> *And humility is the sign of the utmost bitul, the "nothing" of wisdom.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "In the place of His greatness, there you find His humility" (line 328). Humility below is "setting oneself aside", like Moses, "the humblest of men", who did not feel himself at all (line 328). "Humility is the sign of the utmost bitul, the nothing of wisdom"; the more a person is "nothing", the more humble he is (line 328). Step by step: that is why "He gives wisdom to the wise", to those in whom there is bitul; and wisdom "is not found among the haughty", nor in one "who makes himself wide as the sea" (line 328). Above, His contraction to become a source of wisdom is "a great humility", and that humility is what makes His greatness flow out to others (line 329). The text also offers the plainer reading: the greater one is, the lower he can bend (line 329). The text's picture of a new idea: it flashes like lightning, and above it hovers the power that sends it, "hovering over the mind" (line 330). The flash below is the revealed point; the hovering source is hidden, not gone (lines 330-331). Bring it home. When you want to understand something in Torah, first let go of your need to be the one who knows. The text says humility is not a price for wisdom; it is the opening wisdom comes through. The picture of Moses breaks for most of us: we will not become that humble. But each small letting-go makes a little room.

**From inside.** A softening of the chest. Less need to be right. Often, surprisingly, clearer thinking.

**At the edge.** It points there: His humility is "in proportion to the true nothing in His Essence" (line 329). The mind can practice humility; the Nothing it reflects is beyond it.

**Into the day.** In one conversation today, let someone else be right, and notice the room it makes.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §64: "He keeps kindness for thousands": the will becomes a pipe (lines 332-334)

**The movement.** It shows how something immeasurable reaches something small: through a narrow pipe. The word "notzer" (keeps) has the letters of "tzinor" (pipe) and "ratzon" (will). The mind is asked to see the narrowness of what reaches it as the shape of love.

> אך אור רצון זה מתצמצם במאד ונעשה ממנו מקור לחכמה ביוד דאלף כנ"ל שמפלא נעשה אלף כך מרצון נעשה צינור
> *But this light of will contracts itself greatly and becomes a source for wisdom in the yud of the aleph: as wonder becomes aleph, so will becomes a pipe.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: how can there be a leap from His Essence to a source of wisdom at all? "He keeps kindness for thousands" (line 332). "Notzer", keeps, has the letters of "tzinor", pipe, and of "ratzon", will (line 332). The will above is part of His very self; it narrows greatly and becomes a pipe (line 332). Step by step, the picture: pouring from a huge barrel into a small cup. You need a thin spout, or the cup cannot receive (line 332). Or a jug dipped into the great sea: it takes only what it can hold (line 332). Or water rising through thin veins in the earth from the deep, a sealed spring until it breaks out as a river (line 332). And the channels that feed wisdom are like hairs, thin and hollow, growing from the overflow of the mind (line 333). The text works through contradictions: whether these channels feed the hidden wisdom or only the revealed one. It concludes that both readings are true, each speaking of a different level (lines 333-334). Bring it home. What reaches you from God is narrow: a verse, a moment, a small insight. The text says the narrowness is the pipe, shaped so you can receive it. Behind the pipe is the whole barrel. The picture breaks: a pipe is made by someone else. Here the will narrows itself.

**From inside.** Contentment with a small portion, joined to a sense of great depth behind it. Some feel a patience that is not resignation.

**At the edge.** It points there: the will above is "reckoned as the Essence", beyond being any source at all (line 332). The mind receives from the spout; the barrel is beyond sight.

**Into the day.** Take one small insight or verse today and hold it as one drop from a great barrel.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §65: the overflow; R. Akiva's tears (lines 335-338)

**The movement.** It says that what bursts out of a full vessel, the overflow, the crack, the tears, is sometimes rooted higher than what the vessel holds. R. Akiva wept because his mind could not contain the delight of a secret. The mind is taught to respect the overflow.

> כמו בר"ע שזלגו עיניו דמעו' מדחיקת המוח שלא הי' יכול להכיל העונג שבשכל זה
> *As with R. Akiva, whose eyes streamed with tears from the pressure on his mind, which could not contain the delight of this understanding.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the aleph's shape has a yud above, a line, and a yud below: wisdom in wisdom, its feelings, and its giving (line 335). A flash of insight, its leaning, and its passing on into understanding (line 335). Step by step, the overflow. A barrel filled beyond its measure cracks, and water bursts out through the cracks; what bursts out is exactly what the barrel could not hold (line 336). A sage whose wisdom grows beyond his mind's vessel pours out "overflow" (line 336). Dawn breaks through the thick dark sky (line 336). So the Sages call the Torah "the overflow of the higher wisdom", and it is praise: its root is higher than the wisdom itself (line 336). "As with R. Akiva, whose eyes streamed with tears from the pressure on his mind, which could not contain the delight of this understanding" (line 336). His tears came from "the source of the source" of that secret (line 336). "Wisdom gives life to its owner": it reaches the soul's deepest surrounding, its yechidah (line 336). Bring it home. Remember a moment when something too beautiful or too true made your eyes fill. The text says those tears may come from higher than the thought that caused them. The barrel picture breaks: a cracked barrel is damaged. Here the crack is how the higher light gets through.

**From inside.** Tenderness toward your own overflow: tears in prayer, a sigh, a song you cannot stop. They stop feeling like a loss of control and start feeling like a sign.

**At the edge.** Yes. The tears' root is "the source of the source", "the surrounding of the surrounding of the yechidah" (line 336). The mind breaks there; that is the point.

**Into the day.** If tears or a sigh come in prayer today, do not hide them from yourself. Let them be part of the prayer.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §66: aleph, chet, dalet; teaching is complete when it is received (lines 339-341)

**The movement.** It follows the letters of "echad" as a lesson in teaching: the giver, the receiver, and speech. It says a lesson is not yet a lesson until someone has received it, and a wisdom is not yet whole until it can speak and repair. The mind is turned toward the one who receives.

> שזהו עיקר ענין הלימוד כשנתקבל במקבל דוקא
> *For this is the main point of learning: when it has been received by the receiver.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the aleph is the giver's teaching; the chet is the wisdom once received; the large dalet is the power of speech (lines 339-340). "The main point of learning is when it has been received by the receiver"; a teacher who gives and is not received is not yet complete (line 339). Step by step: the chet is shaped like a doorway, lintel and two posts, open for going in and out (line 340). So received wisdom is a door: others come in through it, and the light goes out through it (line 340). "She opens her mouth with wisdom": the mouth is the door (line 340). And "who gave man a mouth? Is it not I, Anochi?" (line 341). The mouth that can sort good from bad, the Oral Torah that rules "fit" and "unfit", is given from the very Essence (line 341). The text's picture: a great scholar who cannot earn a living by his wisdom or fix what is broken is not truly wise; "wisdom gives life to its owner" (line 341). So "Torah is his craft": the spoken Torah that repairs the world gives life even to the root of his wisdom (line 341). "Not by bread alone, but by all that comes from God's mouth" (line 341). Bring it home. When you learn something today, do not stop at understanding it. Say it to someone. Let it pass through the doorway. The picture of the doorway breaks: a door stands still. This door is a mouth, alive, and given by Him.

**From inside.** A shift of attention from "do I understand?" to "can I give it?". A pleasure in explaining, and in hearing that someone else got it.

**At the edge.** It points there: the mouth is given by "Anochi, the Infinite light in the essence of the crown" (line 341). The mind can speak; the gift of speech comes from beyond it.

**Into the day.** Teach one thing you learned today to someone else, even in one sentence.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §67: no two alike; many parts make many blends (lines 342-345)

**The movement.** It asks why no two stones, plants, animals or people are alike, and traces it to the endless combinations of the letters of God's speech. Then it solves a riddle: how the joining of the sefirot makes more division, not less. The mind is led to see every difference in creation as a word no one else could be.

> ולזה אנו רואים בבחי' התחלקו' המדרגות בנבראי' שאין כל א' דומה לחבירו הגם שהוא במין א'
> *This is why we see, in the division of levels among creatures, that no one is like another, even within one kind.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the Sefer Yetzirah says five stones build a hundred and twenty houses, and "from here on, go and count what the mouth cannot say" (line 342). Every angel, every creature, comes from its own letter and its own combination (line 343). "No one is like another, even within one kind": no two stones, no two blades of grass, no two animals, no two minds (line 343). Step by step, the riddle: the sefirot are joined to each other, kindness inside severity and severity inside kindness. How can joining produce more division (line 343)? The text answers with a picture: cut something into many small pieces, and you can blend them in many more ways; blend many things together, and you can then divide the blend in many more ways. "One hangs on the other, measure for measure" (lines 343-344). So "no hour is like another", for in every hour there is a new combination of God's name (line 344). "Generation goes and generation comes", and in each the light is renewed; no generation is like another (line 344). And the root of all this variety is wisdom, which holds everything as one before it divides (lines 344-345). Bring it home. Look at the next person you meet and think: there has never been a combination like this before. Then look at this hour: it is also a new word. The picture of blending breaks: a mixture is made by someone mixing. Here the variety comes out of a unity that never stopped being one.

**From inside.** Wonder at difference. Less comparing yourself to others; you are a different word. Some feel each hour grow fresher.

**At the edge.** It points to wisdom "that includes everything", "You made them all in wisdom", above division and blending (lines 344-345). The mind counts the combinations; it cannot count their source.

**Into the day.** Today, when someone differs from you, say inwardly: "A different letter of the same word."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §68: joy and bitterness from the same thought; the power of speech (lines 346-348)

**The movement.** It notices that two people praying over the same idea are moved differently, one to joy, one to bitterness. It traces this down through the mind to wisdom, where joy and bitterness are not yet separate. Then it separates the power to speak from the words spoken.

> יש מתפעל בחדוה ויש מתפעל במרירות וגם שהתפעלות שניהם מדבר א'
> *One is moved to joy and one is moved to bitterness, though both are moved by one thing.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "God weighs hearts", "God weighs spirits" (line 346). In the service of the heart, "one is moved to joy and one is moved to bitterness, though both are moved by one thing" (line 346). The heart's feelings follow the mind's feelings; minds, too, are not equal (line 346). Step by step, upward: in understanding (binah) each feeling already has its place, like a baby in its mother (line 346). But in the point of wisdom, "sight", there is no difference at all between joy and bitterness. Everything is there as one simple power, like looking at a thing and seeing all its parts at once, with no part first (line 346). Then speech: the power to speak is not the words. A person could speak a thousand years and never use up his power of speech (line 347). A small child cannot speak until the spiritual letters come into his soul; then his mouth moves (line 347). Words are a garment; the power is the self (lines 347-348). Bring it home. If you pray and feel bitter while your friend feels joy over the same verse, the text does not say one of you is wrong. Both come from the same seeing, which holds both. The picture of the glance breaks: a glance still happens in time. The wisdom it points to holds everything before time.

**From inside.** Permission to feel what you feel in prayer. Less comparing. A sense that joy and bitterness can both be doors.

**At the edge.** It points there: in the point of wisdom joy and bitterness are "all as one, really", "a simple, primal power" (line 346). The mind feels one or the other; where they are one is beyond feeling.

**Into the day.** When your feeling in prayer today differs from what you expected, accept it and say: "This too comes from one seeing."

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §69: "Your word stands in the heavens"; speech comes first, wisdom comes last (lines 349-351)

**The movement.** It finishes the letters of "echad" (aleph, chet, dalet) as wisdom, feeling and speech, all one in God's essence. Then it gives a human picture: a child speaks first, feels later, and grows wise last, though in essence wisdom is first. The mind is taught that the order of growth is the reverse of the order of the soul.

> ובאמת זהו פלא שנשלם כח הדבור תחלה ונק' אדם קטן והמדות אח"כ והחכמה באחרונה
> *And truly this is a wonder: that the power of speech is completed first, called "small man", then the feelings afterward, and wisdom last.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Forever, God, Your word stands in the heavens" (line 349). The spoken combinations change endlessly; the power of speech itself does not change (line 349). The heart's openness is its measure: when the heart is tight, speech is short; when it opens, speech widens (line 349). Step by step: all three letters of "echad" are in the "higher unity", where His essence and His life are one (line 350). Aleph is wisdom's hidden root, chet is wisdom holding the feelings, the large dalet is speech's power. All are "above division", joined in the simple Infinite (line 350). In a person too, the power of wisdom, feeling and speech are one in the soul's essence, "not noticed as anything" (line 351). The picture: a child of two tastes grain and can say "Abba"; that is the "small man" of speech. At six the feelings begin to grow; at thirteen the wisdom of the feelings is complete; at twenty wisdom itself; at seventy, "an old man who has acquired wisdom" (line 351). "Truly this is a wonder": speech first, feeling next, wisdom last. "Whatever is higher comes later" in growth, but in essence the order is reversed (line 351). Bring it home. If your spiritual life seems to be mostly words right now, do not despise them. Growth begins with words; feeling and wisdom come later. And the wisdom was there first, underneath. The picture of growing up breaks at the end: we grow old. The essence does not; it was complete before we spoke.

**From inside.** Patience with your own stage. The words you say in prayer feel less empty, more like the beginning of something. A hope for the later fruit.

**At the edge.** It points there: all three letters are "in the utmost unity and bitul before the essence of the Infinite", above division and blending (line 350). The mind can grow in stages; the unity it grows toward was already whole.

**Into the day.** Say one blessing slowly today, trusting that the words will grow into feeling and then into wisdom.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §70: "I am first and I am last"; the vav that runs straight down (lines 352-355)

**The movement.** It turns from "echad" to "va'ed", the same letters exchanged: the higher unity carried down. The aleph becomes a vav, a straight line from top to bottom. The mind is shown a light that is exactly as present in this coarse world as in the highest, because it comes hidden.

> גם בעוה"ז הנפרד כמו בעולם האצי' ממש
> *Even in this separate world, just as in the world of Atzilut itself.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "I am first and I am last" (line 353). "Ani", I, has the letters of "ayin", nothing: the light of the crown. It shines in the end, in Malchut, exactly as in the head, "the I of the crown is the I of Malchut, with no change at all" (line 353). "I fill the heavens and the earth": no place is empty of Him (line 353). Step by step, the riddle: if this light came down revealed, it would dim step by step, as mind dims into feeling and feeling into deed (line 354). So it must come hidden. A hidden light does not dim; it comes straight down like the shape of a vav, the same in all worlds, "even in this separate world, just as in Atzilut itself" (line 354). That is why every creature, high or low, has bitul, not by rank (line 354). The soul's picture: your simple will. It is in your hand's movement as fully as in your thinking, because it is above both. You do not feel it; you think your mind moves you (lines 354-355). And the penitent before his heart wakes: everything that will come out of him is already there, hidden, surrounding (line 355). Bring it home. You may feel that God is closer in a holy place and further in your kitchen or your office. The text says the surrounding light is equally here, now, only hidden. The picture of the will breaks: your will is yours and limited. His surrounding light holds all worlds equally.

**From inside.** A leveling. The ordinary room stops feeling second-class. Some feel a quiet, even presence that does not depend on mood.

**At the edge.** Yes. It is "the Essence of the Infinite light before the first tzimtzum" resting in the general crown (line 353). The mind can know it is here; it cannot see it, by design.

**Into the day.** In the most ordinary place you are in today, say: "Here as in Atzilut."

**Ladder.** L04 (Held from inside and all around)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §71: why the chet becomes an ayin; the light too strong to be shown (lines 356-359)

**The movement.** It explains why the inner light of wisdom does dim as it comes down, unlike the surrounding light. Revealed light must shrink, or the receivers would vanish. The mind is shown that the hiding in our world is also a kindness that keeps us in existence.

> ואם היה מתגלה בבריאה מפנימית חכמ' דאצי' היו מתבטלים ממציאו' לגמרי
> *And if the inner wisdom of Atzilut were revealed in Beriah, they would be nullified out of existence entirely.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the aleph becomes a vav because the surrounding light comes hidden and does not dim (line 356). But the chet, wisdom revealed, "from nothing to something", must dim step by step, changing from one kind to another (lines 356-357). So only the outside of wisdom comes down; its inside stays in Atzilut (line 357). Step by step: "If the inner wisdom of Atzilut were revealed in Beriah, they would be nullified out of existence entirely", for it is beyond the size of their vessels (line 357). The picture: "He stretched out His little finger", the small point of wisdom, and those it touched could not bear it and ceased (line 357). "You made them all in wisdom" means only wisdom's outside dresses itself in the worlds (line 357). Yet in a hidden way, inner wisdom does come down, to sort the sparks in the lowest places: "My glory is behind", dressed there completely hidden, like a person whose wisdom stands by him to earn a living (line 358). And a gloss: in a man's growth, the ayin is the wisdom that serves the feelings, the wisdom of a judge at seventy (line 359). Bring it home. When you wish you could simply see God, remember the little finger. The hiding that frustrates you is also what lets you exist to seek Him. The picture breaks: a finger belongs to a body. The text uses it only to say how small a touch was too much.

**From inside.** Gratitude even for the veil. A calmer longing, without resentment. Some feel held by the very hiding.

**At the edge.** Yes. Inner wisdom is where "the Essence of the Infinite" is (line 357). The mind stays in the outside of wisdom, and is kept alive by that.

**Into the day.** When God feels hidden today, say: "This hiding keeps me here to serve You."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §72: the eye of wisdom; seeing the face of your teacher (lines 360-362)

**The movement.** It studies sight as the outside of wisdom: the wise one "sees what will be born". Then it finds a deeper sight, the gaze of love, that carries the whole delight of the soul. The mind is led from reasoning to beholding.

> שהאריז"ל הי' משיג סודות ורזין דאורייתא בחלום הלילה בשעה א'
> *For the Arizal would grasp secrets of the Torah in a dream at night, in one hour.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: sight belongs to wisdom, hearing to understanding (line 360). "Who is wise? One who sees what will be born": a wise person sees with the eye of the mind what is still hidden, the craft hidden in a person, the good and bad mixed in his heart, "as if he saw it with his eyes" (line 360). Still, this seeing is the outside of wisdom (line 360). Step by step: there is an inner sight. The gaze between those who love each other carries the whole delight of the soul, more than any other joining (line 361). That is why a person must go to see his teacher's face on the festival (line 361). The text's example: "the Arizal would grasp secrets of the Torah in a dream at night, in one hour", things it would take years to write down; this is not reasoning but a spiritual seeing (line 361). "My heart has seen much wisdom" (line 361). Then a turn to speech: what the mind truly grasps, the mouth cannot change; one can speak against one's heart, but not against one's thinking, because speech is built from thought's letters (line 362). Bring it home. There is a time for figuring things out, and a time for simply looking. In prayer today, stop reasoning for a moment and simply behold: "He is here, and He sees me." The picture of the Arizal's dream breaks for us: that seeing was his. But the difference between reasoning and beholding is open to anyone.

**From inside.** A shift from strained thought to open looking. A warmth like meeting someone you love. Some feel seen in return.

**At the edge.** It points there: inner delight in wisdom is "hidden in the nothing of wisdom, beyond grasp and feeling" (line 361). The mind can look; what it sees is beyond its reasoning.

**Into the day.** Look at the face of someone you love or learn from today, and take in who they are before you speak.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §73: the ayin of "va'ed"; the King who rides a light cloud into Egypt (lines 363-366)

**The movement.** It explains the ayin as the feelings of understanding carried into Malchut, built mostly from severity, and yet sustained by kindness. It pictures God riding a light cloud into Egypt: hiding Himself inside exile. The mind is led to find Him hidden in the hard places, holding up the world.

> הנה ה' רוכב על עב קל ובא מצרים שזהו בחי' הסתר אלקו' בגלות
> *"Behold, God rides on a light cloud and comes to Egypt": this is the hiding of Godliness in exile.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the ayin is the seven feelings of understanding, the "large ayin" of "Shema"; from there all feelings flow "from the hidden world to the revealed" (line 363). "Blessed is God from world to world" (line 363). Malchut, like her mother, is built chiefly from the powers of severity (lines 363-364). Step by step: ayin in number is 130, "kal", light: only the five severities, without the balancing of knowledge (lines 364-365). So God "rides on a light cloud and comes to Egypt": the hiding of Godliness in the exile of seventy nations, riding at night, as in a sleep (line 365). Yet the text will not let severity have the last word. "The throne is established by kindness": "if not for the kindness in Malchut, there would be no light or flow of Godliness in the worlds at all" (line 365). And the severities themselves are for the world's sake: "if not for the fear of the kingdom, a man would swallow his fellow"; the King's awe keeps the world standing (line 365). Bring it home. When you are in your own "Egypt", a hard time, a narrow place, the text says He came there riding a light cloud, hidden, and it is His kindness that keeps anything alive there at all. The picture of the rider breaks: a rider can dismount and leave. This One chose to go down into the exile with us.

**From inside.** Company in dark times. A sense that the hardness around you is held by something kinder underneath. Some feel less alone.

**At the edge.** It points to the root of Malchut in "the wisdom of the Father", which the text asks about and leaves for the next sections (line 366). The mind can find Him hidden in Egypt; how the hidden and the kind are one is still ahead.

**Into the day.** In one hard moment today, say: "He rode here on a light cloud; He is here."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §74: love from far, fear from near (lines 367-369)

**The movement.** It brings the letters into the work of prayer. Love grows from distance, fear from nearness, and the furthest love of all, beyond reason, finds God as present below as above. The mind is taught to use both the distance and the nearness it feels.

> עד שנעשה כאבן דומם בלי הרגשת עצמו כלל
> *Until he becomes like a silent stone, without feeling himself at all.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: love and fear are opposites. Love is stirred by distance: the further the beloved, the stronger the longing, like a father for a son far away (line 367). Fear is stirred by nearness: the closer one comes to the king, the more one trembles, "his knees knock", "until he becomes like a silent stone, without feeling himself at all" (line 367). "Yir'ah", fear, has the letters of "re'iyah", seeing (line 367). Step by step: a gloss notes that elsewhere it is the reverse, and resolves it: here the text speaks of love and fear from the giver's side; elsewhere from the receiver's (line 368). Both stand. Then the depth: love from distance can reach self-giving beyond reason, "with all your might" (line 368). The more one grasps how far He is beyond grasp, the more the soul breaks out of its limits. And there, "the strength of His light is found below, in the distance, just as above, in the closest nearness" (line 368). Moses was told to see the land "from afar"; "from afar God appeared to me" (line 369). Bring it home. If today you feel far from God, the text says use it: let the distance become longing. If you feel near, let the nearness become awe. Neither feeling is wasted. The picture of the silent stone breaks: a stone feels nothing. The one who stands before the King is fully alive, only not feeling himself.

**From inside.** Distance turns from despair to yearning. Nearness turns from comfort to reverence. Some feel both at once and are not confused.

**At the edge.** Yes. The love of the distance reaches the crown, "above all grasp", where "below and above are equal" (line 368). The mind cannot close the distance; the longing crosses it.

**Into the day.** When you feel far from God today, say: "From afar God appeared to me," and let yourself long.

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)
