## Shaar Kriat Shema §75: the Torah comes down in a parable's clothing (lines 370-373)

**The movement.** It asks how Torah, rooted in God's inner wisdom, can be here in our hands if inner wisdom cannot come down revealed. The answer is the parable: a deep light reaches even a child when it comes dressed in a foreign garment. The mind is taught to love the garment.

> וכל אור שהוא מן העצמות ממש א"א שיבא למטה בגלוי כמו שהוא כ"א ע"י התלבשות בענין לבוש זר
> *And any light that is from the Essence itself cannot come down revealed as it is, except by dressing in a foreign garment.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the Torah's root is God's inner wisdom, and learning it for its own sake draws the Infinite light down "below as above" (line 370). Three are bound: Israel to the Torah, the Torah to the Holy One (line 370). Yet inner wisdom cannot be revealed below. How can both be true (line 370)? Step by step: "any light that is from the Essence itself cannot come down revealed as it is, except by dressing in a foreign garment" (line 370). The picture: a deep idea that a student would not grasp in forty years of words, he grasps at once through a parable; even a small child can receive it (line 371). The parable is not matter to a form; it is only a garment. So the great and the small hold the same light, each at his depth (line 371). Solomon's parables are great because the wisdom inside them is deepest (line 371). So the Torah of Atzilut and the Torah of this world are one Torah in different garments (lines 371-372). And the dalet does not change letters because speech itself comes down, like a spring that becomes a river (line 372). "I am God, I have not changed" (line 373). Bring it home. When a law about oxen or a story about a well seems plain, remember: this is a parable's clothing, and the deepest light is inside it, the same light given to the greatest sage. The picture of the parable breaks: a parable is made up. The Torah's garments are real laws and real deeds, and the light inside them is not made up either.

**From inside.** Respect for the simple parts of Torah. A childlike joy in learning, knowing the whole light is there. Some feel equal with great scholars in what they hold.

**At the edge.** Yes. The garment hides "the light of the Essence", which the parable cannot show directly (line 370). The mind holds the garment; the light inside is more than it can see.

**Into the day.** Learn one simple verse or law today and say: "The whole light is inside this garment."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §77: counsel in the mind; "God will be witness" (lines 377-379)

**The movement.** It follows how a feeling in the mind is weighed before it becomes a thought, and how the day above is weighed hour by hour. Then it explains the large ayin and dalet, "ed", witness: a witness is needed only for what is hidden. The mind is led to God as the One who sees what no one else can.

> ה' יהי' עד בדבר לפי שהוא יודע כל הנסתרות
> *"God will be witness in the matter", because He knows all hidden things.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: before a feeling in the mind becomes words of thought, it is weighed, like counsel: how much to hide, how much to reveal, until a decision falls (line 377). What is weighed there comes into speech exactly (line 378). So above: "the day has twelve hours". In the first three He sits and studies Torah; then He judges; then He feeds the whole world, and so each day differs (line 378). Step by step, the witness. Why are the ayin of "Shema" and the dalet of "echad" large together? They spell "ed", witness, "God is witness among you" (line 379). And a witness is needed only for what is hidden: for the new moon hiding as a point, not for the sun at noon (line 379). "Man says of a thing hidden from everyone, 'God will be witness in the matter', because He knows all hidden things", "the secrets of every living being are revealed before Him" (line 379). So Binah, the hidden world, witnesses to Israel, the revealed one (line 379). Bring it home. There are things in your heart no one knows: a good you did quietly, a struggle you never told. The text says there is a Witness to exactly these. The picture of a witness in court breaks: a court witness can lie or forget. This Witness knows from inside.

**From inside.** Being known. Some feel relief; some feel a sober honesty. Either way the heart stops performing.

**At the edge.** It points to "the hidden world", which alone can witness what is hidden (line 379). The mind cannot see its own depths fully; it can know it is seen.

**Into the day.** Do one good thing today that no one will know about, and say: "You are witness."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §78: the small letters of "shem"; going down at night to gather sparks (lines 380-383)

**The movement.** It asks why the shin and mem of "Shema" are small. Malchut, the "name", goes down small, at night, in hiding, to gather the fallen sparks, and rises with them. The mind is shown a greatness that makes itself small to lift what fell, and a future where the evil is gone as if it never was.

> שזהו לאחר תשלום כל הברורים דוקא שנהפך הרע לטוב כאלו לא הי' במציאת מעולם
> *This is only after all the sorting is complete, when evil is turned to good as if it had never existed.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "shem", name, is Malchut: "David made a name" (line 381). Malchut goes down into the lower worlds to sort the 288 sparks that fell in the breaking; sparks plus her name "ban" add up to "shem" (line 381). Whoever sorts must dress in the garment of what is sorted, so she goes down small (line 381). Step by step: "She rises while it is still night and gives food", hidden in the halls of mixed good and evil, like one who goes to a dark mountain and brings back food (line 381). By the sorting, "turning darkness to light and subduing the other side, the glory of the Holy One rises", as a king gains a great name by conquering the farthest lands (line 382). Even when she rises, she stays an open mem, because she rises with the sparks, and the outside still has a root there (line 383). Only in the end, "after all the sorting is complete, when evil is turned to good as if it had never existed", "He will swallow death forever" (line 383). Bring it home. When you find yourself in a low place, among coarse things, doing plain work, you may be the one gathering a spark there. Smallness is how the gathering is done. The picture of the king conquering breaks: conquest destroys. Here the far places are not destroyed but turned to light.

**From inside.** Dignity in humble work. Patience with the night. Some feel hope for a time when even their failures will be as if they never were.

**At the edge.** It points to the end, the inner Binah, "where there is no root for evil at all" (line 383). The mind works in the night; the day when evil never was is beyond its sight.

**Into the day.** Do one small, plain task today as if you were gathering a spark, and say: "For Your name."

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §79: "Whoever makes this unity every day, joy awaits him"; the upper becomes lower (lines 384-386)

**The movement.** It reads the Zohar's promise of joy to the one who unifies each day, and asks what makes this unity new. In the Shema the order is turned around: the higher gives downward and the lower rises up to receive from the highest. The mind is shown a unity so full that above and below trade places.

> כל מאן דמייחד יחוד' דא בכל יומא חדוה זמינא לי' מלעילא
> *Whoever makes this unity every day, joy awaits him from above.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the Zohar says the small letters left over, shin-mem and aleph-chet, spell "ashmach", "I will rejoice in God" (line 384). "Whoever makes this unity every day, joy awaits him from above" (line 384). So this unity must be something new and wonderful, to bring such joy (line 384). Step by step: usually the feelings of the heart give to speech, like a voice to words; this is the familiar union (line 385). Speech without the heart's feeling is lifeless, "like physical letters, matter without form" (line 385). But in the Shema there is a higher union: the feelings of the mind come down to meet speech, and speech rises to receive from the mind (line 386). "Upper becomes lower from the side of the giver, and lower becomes upper from the side of the receiver" (line 386). The order that always was is reversed, and that newness is the source of the joy (line 386). Bring it home. When you say the Shema today, let your words be not only the end of your thoughts; let them reach up. Let what you say lift your mind, and your mind bend down into what you say. The picture of the voice and word breaks: a voice cannot be lifted by its word. In the Shema the lower truly lifts the higher.

**From inside.** A living quality in the words of prayer. A joy that feels given, not produced. Some feel the Shema as something new, not recited.

**At the edge.** It points toward the joy "from above", from a union "more wondrous" than the usual order (line 384). The mind can say the words; the joy is waiting for it, not made by it.

**Into the day.** Say the Shema today as if doing something new for the first time, and wait for the joy.

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §80: "The glory of God is to hide a thing"; the silence of one thinking hard (lines 387-390)

**The movement.** It gives a plain human picture for the higher union: a scholar struggling with a question of law goes silent, then his lips move without sound, drawn after his thinking. The mind learns that speech rising into silence is itself a glory.

> הוא בא לבחי' השתיקה ששותק מלדבר מאומה עד שיוציא לאור האמת במדות שבשכל
> *He comes to silence, saying nothing at all, until he brings the truth to light in the feelings of the mind.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "The glory of God is to hide a thing" (line 387). Usually thought receives from the heart constantly; "there is no moment without thought" (line 387). That is nothing new. The large dalet here is not ordinary thinking but "the letters of understanding", the thought in which a real insight is dressed (line 388). Step by step, the picture: a person troubled by a question of Torah, whether a thing is forbidden or permitted, cannot yet find the truth. "He comes to silence, saying nothing at all, until he brings the truth to light" (line 389). Even then he does not answer aloud; his lips move quietly, not feeling what he says, his speech pulled after his deep thinking (line 389). "My Lord, open my lips" (line 389). So Malchut, speech, rises silently into understanding, hidden from all sides: hidden in the height it rose to, and cut off from the voice below. "This is the glory of God: to hide a thing" (line 390). Bring it home. When you do not know the answer, in Torah or in life, let yourself go quiet instead of talking. The text says the silence is not empty; speech is rising to its source. The picture breaks gently: a scholar's silence ends when he finds the answer. This silence is also a union, and does not need to end quickly.

**From inside.** A dignified quiet. Lips that move without sound. A patience for truth to come. Some feel their prayer grow deeper as it grows quieter.

**At the edge.** It points there: speech rising to "the essence of the light of the mind", beyond voice (line 389). The mind can be silent; what fills the silence is beyond words.

**Into the day.** When you face a hard question today, stay silent a little longer than usual before you answer.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §81: "chedvah", hidden joy; "ashmach", I will rejoice in God (lines 391-397)

**The movement.** It explains why this union brings joy: the lower rises higher than the higher. It separates open gladness from hidden joy, the joy of good news held inside. Then it reads the letters "ashmach", and the tefillin, as the place where that joy is bound. The mind is led to an inner joy that shows nothing and holds everything.

> כמו שיגיל האדם מבשורה טובה בתוך פנימי' המוח והלב בלתי נגלה ממנו שום התפעלות
> *As a person rejoices at good news, inside the mind and heart, with no outward feeling showing.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: in this union Malchut rises higher than Z'A: the receiver goes above the giver (line 391). Below, the giver is higher; above, "the reverse" (line 391). And the joy that awaits is "chedvah", not "simcha": simcha spreads outward into feeling and speech; chedvah stays hidden in the inside of mind and heart (line 392). Step by step, the picture: "as a person rejoices at good news, inside the mind and heart, with no outward feeling showing"; when he wants to show it, he makes a feast (line 392). And a king used to every treasure is moved only by something new and wonderful, the more so by something turned end to end, from upper to lower (line 392). The upside-down union is that new thing, and it brings Him joy. The letters "ashmach": the name (shin-mem) stands between aleph and chet, enclosed on both sides, "for there is no truer union than this", "I will rejoice in God", in God Himself (line 393). The tefillin tie these minds; and "a person is moved only by letters of thought", only when contemplation takes the form of clear letters (line 397). Bring it home. Think of a time you received good news and kept it inside for a while. The text says there is a joy like that in the Shema, too deep to show. The picture of the feast breaks: a feast ends. The hidden joy the text speaks of is "in its place", in the Essence (line 392).

**From inside.** A quiet gladness underneath everything. Nothing to show and no need to show it. Some feel a secret smile inside prayer.

**At the edge.** Yes. The hidden joy "is drawn from the joy in its place, from the Essence of the Emanator" (line 392). The mind can hold the good news; the Joy itself is His.

**Into the day.** When something good happens today, keep it inside for a while before telling anyone, and let it become thanks.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §83: the tefillin straps; prophets who speak without feeling themselves (lines 398-401)

**The movement.** It reads the straps of the tefillin as the "counsel" that carries the mind's light down, and then turns to the root of prophecy. A prophet is one in whom the Essence itself speaks, "without his feeling himself at all". The mind is shown the far end of bitul: a mouth that is only His.

> אבל הנביא הוא שבו נגלה עצמו' אור האלקי דלית בי' מחשבה והשגה
> *But the prophet is one in whom the very essence of Godly light is revealed, which no thought or grasp can hold.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: two straps go out from the head tefillin, right and left, like the thighs that carry a body from place to place (line 398). They are the counsel of the mind: how, how much and in what way the light should come down into thought (lines 398-399). Every counsel holds a thing and its opposite, a yes and a no (line 399). Step by step: a wise man grasps with his mind, but "the prophet is one in whom the very essence of Godly light is revealed, which no thought or grasp can hold" (line 400). It comes in images, "in a dream I speak with him" (line 400). The prophet must be a vessel purified of all coarseness, and the word of God "speaks in the prophet's mouth, without his feeling himself at all" (line 400). And each prophet receives by his root and his time: one whose soul is rooted in severity, like Jeremiah, comes when judgments rule and the Temple will fall; one rooted in kindness, like Isaiah, comes when kindness shines (line 401). Bring it home. We are not prophets. But the text's picture, a person so transparent that He can speak through him, is a direction. And your own soul's root fits your own time. The picture of the prophet breaks for us: we cannot make prophecy. We can make ourselves more of a vessel.

**From inside.** Acceptance of the time you live in as fitting your soul. A longing to be transparent. Some feel less need to be the author of what they say.

**At the edge.** Yes. What the prophet receives is "the essence of Godly light, which no thought or grasp can hold" (line 400). The mind stops where prophecy begins.

**Into the day.** Say today: "My soul was sent for this time," and do one thing the time asks of you.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §84: "Walk before Me" and "walk after God": two ways of walking (lines 402-404)

**The movement.** It names two directions of spiritual life: the unlimited coming down into the limited, and the limited rising to the unlimited. The picture is a student and a teacher: the teacher's wisdom surrounds the student until the student grows to hold it. The mind learns that the descent is for the sake of the rise.

> וירידה זו שיוגבל הבלתי מוגבל מא"מ לאו"פ יריד' זו רק לצורך עלי'
> *And this descent, where the unlimited becomes limited, from surrounding light to inner light, is only for the sake of an ascent.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "You will see My back": God showed Moses the knot of the tefillin (line 402). The whole Torah is likened to tefillin, because the Torah is the joining of left in right, fire and water, every law a precise blend of yes and no (line 402). The first paragraph of the Shema says "with all your might"; the second does not (line 403). Step by step: to Abraham, "walk before Me"; to Israel, "walk after God" (line 403). One walking draws the surrounding light down into the inner; the other lifts the inner up to the surrounding (line 403). "With all your might", beyond measure, is the surrounding, a love that does not settle in mind or heart (line 403). "And this descent, where the unlimited becomes limited, is only for the sake of an ascent" (line 403). The picture: a great teacher's mind surrounds a small student, who cannot contain it. As the student grows, what surrounded him comes inside; "a student does not reach his teacher's mind" for forty years, and then he does (line 404). The teacher's wisdom did not change; the student's vessel grew. And when the student has grown, his inner has risen to the level of the surrounding, while staying himself (line 404). Bring it home. There are things about God you cannot hold yet. They surround you. The text says keep growing; what surrounds you now will one day be inside. The picture breaks at the top: a student can one day equal his teacher. The Infinite remains unlimited even inside the vessel.

**From inside.** Patience with what you do not yet understand. A sense of being surrounded rather than shut out. Some feel eager to grow.

**At the edge.** Yes. The surrounding light is "not limited in any vessel", and stays unlimited even when it dwells inside (lines 403-404). The mind grows; the Light stays beyond.

**Into the day.** Choose one thing in Torah you do not understand yet and say: "It surrounds me; I will grow into it."

**Ladder.** L04 (Held from inside and all around)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §85: study first, then tefillin; tefillin first, then study (lines 405-407)

**The movement.** It notices that the two paragraphs put Torah study and tefillin in opposite order, and reads the two orders as the two walkings. Torah study draws the Essence down; the deed lifts the world up. The mind learns that sometimes study is higher and sometimes deed, and both are true.

> שהוא גלוי המשכת עצמות הא"ס מלמעלה מעלה למטה מטה באבי"ע
> *It is the revealing and drawing down of the Essence of the Infinite from the highest heights to the lowest depths, through all the worlds.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: in the first paragraph "you shall teach them" comes before "you shall bind them"; in the second, "bind" comes before "teach" (line 405). There are higher mitzvot, kept by the patriarchs in a spiritual way, which add light above (line 405). And there are mitzvot in physical deed, which sort good from evil and lift upward (line 406). Step by step: Torah study is the first walking, from above to below. It is called "halacha", a going, "the ways of the world are His" (line 406). It is "the revealing and drawing down of the Essence of the Infinite from the highest heights to the lowest depths, through all the worlds", mainly in speech: "I have put My words in your mouth"; "the Holy One sits and learns opposite him" (line 406). The Torah was His delight, "I was with Him, His delight", before the world (line 406). So when the drawing is downward, Torah comes first; when the lifting is upward, the deed comes first (line 407). That is why sometimes "Torah study equals them all", and sometimes "not study but deed is the main thing" (line 407). Bring it home. When you learn Torah aloud, picture the Essence coming down through all the worlds into your mouth. When you do a mitzvah, picture the world lifted up. You need both walkings. The picture of a road breaks: a road goes one way at a time. Here the one who walks down gives strength to walk up.

**From inside.** A fuller sense of what learning does. Less competition between study and action. Some feel their words of Torah as a real descent.

**At the edge.** Yes. Torah study reveals "the Essence of the Infinite", and its root is "the first light", hidden in His essence (line 406). The mind can say the words; the drawing down is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Learn a few lines of Torah aloud today, slowly, as if it were coming down through you.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §86: "doing His will"; He desired a home (lines 408-411)

**The movement.** It asks why the second paragraph, which is all about keeping mitzvot, is said to be when Israel does "not" do His will. The answer: "doing His will" means awakening the will above. The deed below does it fully only when the sorting is complete, and then His delight in it will be revealed. The mind is led to the deepest reason for a simple deed.

> יתגלה עצמו' התענוג והרצון שהי' לו בזה כי נתאוה להיות לו
> *The very essence of the delight and will He had in this will be revealed, for He desired to have it.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the Sages say the first paragraph speaks of when Israel does God's will, and the second of when they do not (line 408). That seems strange: the second paragraph speaks of heart and soul, and of mitzvot (line 409). The answer: "doing His will" means making the will above; "He is the place of the world, and the world is not His place" (line 409). Step by step: accepting the yoke of Heaven in the Shema wakes the simple will that stands between the Essence and the worlds, and unites the surrounding and the filling (line 410). The mitzvot in deed sort and lift from below; His will is dressed in them, hidden, for the sake of the lifting (line 410). But when all the sorting is complete, "the very essence of the delight and will He had in this will be revealed, for He desired to have it" (line 410). Then below and above will be as equal as before the tzimtzum, and in this the second paragraph is higher (line 411). Bring it home. When a mitzvah feels like an obligation, remember the reason under it: He desired this. Your small deed today is part of what He wanted from the start. The picture of desire breaks: our desires come from lack. His desire is not a lack; it is His delight choosing.

**From inside.** Tenderness in plain duties. A sense of being wanted. Some feel a quiet pride that their deed is part of His desire.

**At the edge.** Yes. What will be revealed is "the essence of the delight and will", equal "as before the tzimtzum" (lines 410-411). The mind does the deed; the Delight is His.

**Into the day.** Before one mitzvah today, say: "You desired this," and then do it.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §87: "I will walk among you"; this world, where the low can rise (lines 412-414)

**The movement.** It shows what this world has that the next does not: teshuvah, where the lowest can rise to the highest in an hour, and the highest can fall. This is the world of sorting, where God's hand reaches down to the very bottom. The mind is given hope and warning together.

> יש קונה עולמו בשעה א' מפני שעשה תשוב' שלימה
> *One may acquire his world in one hour, because he did complete teshuvah.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: only in this world is there teshuvah. Even a completely wicked person can return fully and become like the completely righteous, "turning evil to good from end to end", until his sins become merits (line 412). "One may acquire his world in one hour", said Rebbi of one who did complete teshuvah and whose soul left him in tears (line 412). Step by step: and the opposite: a great tzaddik can fall entirely, like Yochanan the High Priest who served eighty years and became a Sadducee (line 412). This is the upper coming down and the lower going up (line 412). In this world "Your right hand is stretched out to receive those who return", stretched all the way down (line 413). Why? Because this world is the time of sorting good from evil (line 413). In the World to Come the light comes only to the one prepared for it; "the sun taken out of its sheath" heals the righteous and burns the wicked (line 414). The root is the general surrounding light, before which "upper is lower and lower is upper" (line 413). Bring it home. Wherever you are today, however low, you can rise in one hour. And wherever you are, however high, stay humble. This world is the place of turning. The picture of rising and falling breaks: the text is not about luck. It is about the stretched-out hand, always there.

**From inside.** Hope that feels real, not forced. A sober care about one's own standing. Some feel an urgency to use today.

**At the edge.** Yes. The root is the surrounding light before which upper and lower are the same (line 413). The mind can grasp that it can return; the Hand that receives is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Choose one thing to return from today, and do it within the hour.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §88: the one on the mountain and the one in the valley (lines 415-417)

**The movement.** It gives a simple picture for the two ways of meeting: the one on the mountain comes down, or the one in the valley climbs up. Then it lifts the picture: in God's Essence, like a line with no end, there is no up or down at all. The mind moves from climbing to resting in a light with no top.

> א' עומד למעלה בהר גבוה וא' עומד למטה בבקעה
> *One stands above on a high mountain, and one stands below in a valley.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Two people: one stands above on a high mountain, and one stands below in a valley" (line 415). They can meet in two ways: the one above comes down, or the one below climbs up (line 415). The meeting is the same, but when the high one comes down, anyone can meet him as he is, in his clothes; when the low one climbs, he must become pure to get there (line 415). Step by step: this world is the high one coming down, so even those "in the lowest pit" can receive light and return (line 415). The World to Come is the climb: "Who will ascend the mountain of God? One with clean hands and a pure heart" (line 415). First comes the complete lifting, in the days of Mashiach; then the drawing down from the highest Essence, "below as above, equally" (line 416). Then the picture breaks open: a measured circle has a top and bottom. But an endless line, a light with no measure, has no beginning or end, no up or down (line 417). "A day that is all long, all good, its end like its start" (line 417). There, going down and going up are one; "I will walk among you" (line 417). Bring it home. Today, you do not need to climb to meet Him; He came down to the valley, and you can meet Him in your work clothes. And the climbing you do is also held in a light where there is no far. The picture of the mountain breaks at the end, as the text says: in the Essence there is no mountain and no valley.

**From inside.** Relief from having to be pure before you can come close. A gentle aspiration to climb anyway. Some feel the "up" and "down" of their days soften.

**At the edge.** Yes. "The light of the Essence itself" has no "head or end, no above or below at all" (line 417). The mind thinks in ups and downs; this is beyond it.

**Into the day.** In your ordinary clothes and ordinary place today, say: "You came down to meet me here."

**Ladder.** L08 (What can't meet in me meets in Him)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §89: "I will lead you upright", two statures (lines 418-421)

**The movement.** It reads "komemiyut", upright, as two statures: the light coming down from head to heel, and the light rising from heel to head. Before His Essence the two are one. And when the Essence reveals itself below, it does not shrink; it widens. The mind is led to a receiving as wide as the Giver.

> אבל קמי' עצמותו ממש שניהם כא' שוין
> *But before His very Essence, both are one and equal.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "I will lead you upright", "komemiyut", two statures (line 418). One stature is the light coming straight down, from the head of the first man above to his heels at the bottom of the world; the other is the light returning, from below to above (line 418). They are opposites, "but before His very Essence, both are one and equal" (line 418). Step by step, the human picture: a baby's traits are only points; a child's traits grow each with its own mind; an adult's mind grows full (line 419). The first Adam stood from Beriah to Assiyah; after the sin he was made smaller (line 418). The whole Jewish people is one stature "in His image", including both walkings (line 420). And the essential point: a flow from a giver shrinks as it goes down. But when "the Essence itself is revealed below as above", it does not shrink; "it widens as it comes down" (line 421). The vessel receives the whole breadth of the light. "You will be a land of desire"; "a good and broad land", without measure (line 421). Bring it home. You may think you can only receive a little of God, because you are small. The text says: from a giver, yes; from the Essence, the vessel becomes as wide as the light. The picture of a person's height breaks: a stature has a measure. The stature the text speaks of rises "in the stature of the Essence, truly infinite" (line 421).

**From inside.** Expansion. The feeling of being small softens into a sense of being a wide land. Some feel a new courage in prayer.

**At the edge.** Yes. Rising and falling "are all in the Essence", "where there is no above and below at all" (line 420). The mind stands in one stature; both are one beyond it.

**Into the day.** In prayer today, ask not for a little but for His presence itself, and trust that you can hold it.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §90: the true Nothing dwells only with the lowly (lines 422-424)

**The movement.** It asks whether study or deed is greater, and starts from the root: every mitzvah aims at the union of the Holy One and His Shechinah. Then it names why lowliness draws God: His Essence is the true Nothing and cannot dwell in something puffed up. The mind is taught humility as the only fitting home.

> דבחי' רוממות העצמות הרי הוא בחי' אין האמיתי שלא יוכל לשכון בבחי' יש וגיאות רק בבחי' אין דשפלות
> *For the exaltedness of the Essence is the true Nothing, which cannot dwell in "something" and pride, only in the "nothing" of lowliness.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "this mitzvah", in the singular, means the Shema, which includes all the mitzvot (line 422). It makes the general union of the surrounding and the filling (line 423). "You are One but not in number, wise but not with a known wisdom, not of any of these attributes at all" (line 423). The whole line of light is "as nothing before the Essence" (line 423). Step by step: "God is high and sees the lowly" (line 424). "The exaltedness of the Essence is the true Nothing, which cannot dwell in 'something' and pride, only in the 'nothing' of lowliness" (line 424). "His dwelling is in broken vessels" (line 424). "Where you find His greatness, there you find His humility"; since His greatness has no end, His humility to contract Himself has no end either, and that is His strength: "You are mighty forever" (line 424). A gloss ranks fear above love: the servant's total bitul reaches the Essence more than the most wondrous love, as the penitent's weeping soul shows; so "Moses My servant" (line 424). The text keeps the Zohar's praise of love, and still says this. Bring it home. When you feel broken, the text does not see a damaged vessel. It sees the kind of vessel He dwells in. And when you feel important, it is a moment to step down. The picture of the broken vessel breaks: we do not break ourselves on purpose. The text asks for lowliness, not self-harm.

**From inside.** Tenderness toward your own brokenness. A quiet stepping back from pride. Some feel closer to God exactly when they feel least.

**At the edge.** Yes. His exaltedness "is the true Nothing" (line 424). The mind can be lowly; the Nothing that dwells there is beyond it.

**Into the day.** When you feel small today, say: "His dwelling is in broken vessels," and let it comfort you.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §91: "I want to, but my Father in heaven decreed"; the yoke (lines 425-427)

**The movement.** It names two sides of every mitzvah: setting aside your own will, and taking on a yoke beyond your nature. The yoke, doing something because the King said so, is higher. And these two below awaken His humility and His strength above, bringing His Oneness down into physical things. The mind learns the dignity of doing what is hard.

> אלא אפשי אבל אבי שבשמים גזר עלי
> *Rather: "I want to, but my Father in heaven has decreed upon me."*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: there are two things in a mitzvah (line 425). First, setting aside one's will, "nullify your will before His", like a servant who has no will but his master's (line 425). Second, the yoke: doing more than your nature, "the one who reviews his chapter a hundred and one times" (line 425), giving charity beyond what comes naturally (line 425). Step by step: "Let a person not say 'I do not want pork', but 'I want to, but my Father in heaven has decreed upon me'" (line 426). The yoke seems more external, but it is greater: "Who is mighty? One who conquers his inclination" (line 426). Above, the same two: His humility, setting Himself aside to be a source; and His strength, contracting Himself without limit (line 426). So your bitul of will wakes His humility; your strength against your nature wakes His strength (line 426). And the mitzvot are "the clothing of God's simple Oneness in physical things below" (line 427). "Whoever reads the Shema without tefillin is as if he gave false testimony": without the deed, the union stays only above (line 427). Bring it home. When you do a mitzvah you do not feel like doing, do not think it is worth less. The text says it may be worth more: it is the yoke, the strength, and it wakes His strength. The picture of the servant breaks: a servant may resent his master. The yoke the text speaks of is taken from a Father.

**From inside.** A clean, strong feeling in doing the hard thing. Less need to feel inspired first. Some feel a quiet dignity, like carrying something important.

**At the edge.** It points to His strength "to contract Himself without limit" (line 426). The mind can take on the yoke; how that moves the Infinite is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Do one mitzvah today that you do not feel like doing, and say: "My Father in heaven asked this."

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §92: study or deed; the lamp and the light (lines 428-430)

**The movement.** It takes up the old question of whether study or deed is greater, and holds both. The deed is greater at its root, because it wakes God's contraction; study is greater below, because it is the light that shows how to do the deed. The mind learns not to set its learning against its doing.

> דנר בלא אור אינו כלום כך אין למצוה אור בלא אור דתור' דוקא
> *As a lamp without light is nothing, so a mitzvah has no light without the light of Torah.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the Sages asked: is study greater or deed greater? They concluded: study is greater, "because it leads to deed" (line 428). But then the deed seems the goal. The text answers from two sides (line 428). Step by step: the deed is greater at its root, because doing a mitzvah with bitul and against nature wakes His contraction above (line 428). But the mitzvah's body is shaped by Torah: "a mitzvah is a lamp and Torah is light"; "as a lamp without light is nothing, so a mitzvah has no light without the light of Torah" (line 428). So study is greater below, as the light of the deed; "both are true, one hangs on the other" (line 429). The picture: a king orders a palace built with many rooms; the builders can do nothing without his precise decree for every detail (line 429). The Oral Torah is that decree: fit or unfit, pure or impure (line 429). "Do not read 'your children' but 'your builders'": those who study build the world (line 429). Bring it home. When you learn a law, you are receiving the King's plan for a room in His palace. When you do it, you build the room. Neither without the other. The picture of the palace breaks: a palace is finished once. This building goes on every day.

**From inside.** Unity between head and hands. Less guilt about "only learning" or "only doing". Some feel their learning become practical and their deeds become luminous.

**At the edge.** It points to the root of the mitzvot in "the inner will above", beyond the light of Torah (line 428). The mind can learn the plan; the will behind it is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Learn the details of one mitzvah you will do today, then do it with the light of what you learned.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §93: running and returning; the King's limbs (lines 431-433)

**The movement.** It orders the Shema: love, which runs up; study, which returns down; then tefillin. Then it gives the mitzvot a body: they are the King's limbs, and a sin is like a wound that lets the life-blood leak out to the outside. The mind is given both the pulse of prayer and the seriousness of each deed.

> כדפיקו דלבא שעולה ויורד תמיד
> *Like the beating of the heart, which rises and falls constantly.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "You shall love" comes first, then "you shall teach them", then "you shall bind them" (line 431). Love is "running": "if your heart runs, return", "and the living beings ran and returned" (line 431). The soul rises and longs upward, and then returns and comes down, "like the beating of the heart, which rises and falls constantly" (line 431). Step by step: in the Shema, the divine soul binds itself to His simple Oneness, ready "to give itself up in One" (line 431). But that is not done by running alone; the return is Torah study, drawing the Infinite into the soul through the word of God (line 431). Then the mitzvot as a body: "as if you made Me", 248 positive mitzvot as 248 limbs of the King (lines 432-433). A limb holds the soul's life in its blood; when the vessel is damaged, the life leaks out (line 432). So a sin lets the inner light flow out to the outside forces, and this lengthens the exile (line 433). Bring it home. Your prayer has a pulse: up in longing, down into learning and doing. Do not try to stay only "up". And every mitzvah you keep is a healthy limb; every one you drop is a small wound. The picture of the body breaks, as the text itself says: He has no body. The picture only shows how much each deed holds.

**From inside.** A rhythm instead of strain. A sober love for each mitzvah. Some feel prayer become a heartbeat rather than a performance.

**At the edge.** It points to "His simple Oneness", which the soul binds itself to (line 431). The mind can run and return; the One it runs to is beyond it.

**Into the day.** After a moment of strong feeling in prayer today, "return" by learning one line of Torah.

**Ladder.** L07 (Know it today, bring it to the heart)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §94: blood from the curtain; Torah as healing (lines 434-436)

**The movement.** It reads a hard story: Titus cut the Temple curtain and blood came out. The mitzvot are the King's limbs, and sin let the inner light leak outward. But Torah is the medicine that renews a damaged limb as if it had never been damaged. The mind is led from grief to healing.

> יכול לחזור לאיתנו ולהחתזק בכל כחו הפנימי להתמשך בכחו' חיצוניות באברים כאלו לא היה בהן קלקול מעולם
> *He can return to his strength and draw all his inner power into his outer limbs, as if they had never been damaged.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: when Titus cut the curtain, blood came out, and he thought he had killed Him (line 432). The text reads it as a sign: the Second Temple generation had damaged the vessels of holiness, and the inner light leaked to the outside (line 434). In that hiding, "before His Essence darkness is like light"; even Israel and the nations are equal, which is why we say He "chooses" (line 434). Step by step: why was the Second Temple destroyed for neglecting Torah, if idolatry and bloodshed are worse (line 435)? Because Torah is the light and medicine of all the limbs (line 435). The picture: a person very sick in every limb, whose strength leaves him in a moment, can still, through the right medicines, "return to his strength and draw all his inner power into his outer limbs, as if they had never been damaged" (line 435). And Torah is the head: the brain feels all the limbs, so neglect of Torah damages everything (line 436). Only teshuvah heals even the head; and the self-sacrifice for Torah after the destruction repaired this (line 436). Bring it home. If some part of your spiritual life feels damaged, the text says it is not beyond healing. Learn. Torah draws new life into the very place that was hurt. The picture of medicine breaks: medicine can fail. The text trusts that Torah and teshuvah reach the head itself.

**From inside.** Hope instead of shame. A sense of healing starting from within. Some feel a strong pull to return to learning.

**At the edge.** It points to "the Essence, before whom darkness is like light", where even choosing is needed (line 434). The mind can heal limb by limb; the Chooser is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Pick the part of your life that feels most damaged, and learn one piece of Torah about it today.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §95: now study is greater; in the time to come, the deed (lines 437-439)

**The movement.** It turns the order around: now Torah is the light that shows the deed, but in the time to come the mitzvah, which is pure will without reason, will stand above the Torah. The mind is taught to value the simple deed as rooted higher than understanding.

> אבל לעתיד יהי' להיפך דבר מצוה יהי' למעלה מאור דתור'
> *But in the time to come it will be the reverse: the mitzvah will be above the light of Torah.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: in the first paragraph study comes before tefillin; in the second, tefillin before study (line 437). "Study is greater" only now, because now a mitzvah has light only through Torah (line 437). "But in the time to come it will be the reverse: the mitzvah will be above the light of Torah" (line 437). Step by step: the mitzvot are the higher will; Torah is higher wisdom; and will is above wisdom, "for will has no reason", and no reason can reach the will as it is (line 437). "A woman of valor is her husband's crown": the deed will be a crown above the mind (line 437). The whole second paragraph, the text says, speaks of the future: exile, return, and then deeds that shine (line 438). "The end of the deed rose first in the will of the Essence" (line 439). "Fear God and keep His mitzvot, for this is all of man" (line 439). And in the future the angels will say "holy" before the righteous, because the revealing will be greater below than above (line 439). Bring it home. A simple mitzvah, done without understanding it, is not second-class. The text says it reaches the will, which is higher than any reason you could give. The picture of the crown breaks: a crown sits outside the head. This crown is the will from which the head came.

**From inside.** Respect for simple deeds and simple people. A lightness in doing what you do not fully understand. Some feel close to God in the plainest act.

**At the edge.** Yes. The will is "above the hidden reason", beyond any grasp (line 437). The mind can learn why; the will beyond why is reached only by doing.

**Into the day.** Do one mitzvah today without looking for its reason, simply because He wants it.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §96: "the word of a king is power"; Malchut as the deed of Atzilut (lines 440-442)

**The movement.** It asks why Malchut, the source of all worlds, is called "the deed of Atzilut". The answer is that everything in it, will, wisdom, speech, is only for making. A king's word is a decree that happens. The mind is shown that divine speech is action, and that God's kingship is His word being fulfilled.

> שכל מה שהמלך גוזר כן יקום בפו"מ משא"כ זולת המלך אין דבורו עושה כלום
> *Whatever the king decrees is carried out in actual deed, whereas anyone other than the king, his speech does nothing.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: each of the four worlds contains all four (line 440). Malchut is called "Assiyah of Atzilut", the doing within the divine, because it is the source of making something from nothing (line 440). Even its will and wisdom are the will and wisdom "that belong to making", like a man who decides to build: his thinking, speaking and planning are all "for the deed" (line 441). Step by step: "the word of a king is power": "whatever the king decrees is carried out in actual deed, whereas anyone other than the king, his speech does nothing" (line 442). "He said, let there be a firmament, and it was so" (line 442). "My word that goes out of My mouth will not return empty" (line 442). His kingship is exactly this: His decree stands (line 442). And the Oral Torah is that kind of speech, "the Mishnah is a queen", decreeing how to do (line 442). Words of wisdom or love that do not lead to action are a different kind of speech (line 442). Bring it home. When you accept His kingship in the Shema, you accept that His word happens. And your own words, when they are His commands done, share in that kingship. The picture of the king breaks: a human king's decree needs servants. His word creates by itself.

**From inside.** Trust that what He said will be. Seriousness about your own words. Some feel their promises weigh more.

**At the edge.** It points toward "the King whose kingdom is the kingdom of all worlds" (line 442). The mind sees the decree fulfilled; the Speaker is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Keep one promise today exactly as you said it, as a small echo of His word.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §97: Torah from His own wisdom; the greater the wisdom, the lower it can bend (lines 443-446)

**The movement.** It places the root of Torah in God's own primordial wisdom, above good and evil, and says that is exactly why it can come down to sort good from evil in the lowest places. The mind is shown a wisdom whose height is measured by how low it can reach.

> יותר יוכל להשפיל כח חכמתו לתקן היותר מקולקל
> *The more he can lower the power of his wisdom to repair what is most broken.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Torah comes out of higher wisdom", yet its laws seem to belong to the feelings, the "measures" of kindness and severity (line 443). The text holds both: the Torah's root is His own primordial wisdom, "I was with Him, His delight" (line 444). It came down through the worlds until the revealed Torah we have (line 444). It is "a tree of life", above the tree of good and evil, and yet its laws separate good from evil (line 444). Step by step: is that not a great descent? The answer is a picture of a great sage: the greater his wisdom, "the more he can lower the power of his wisdom to repair what is most broken" (line 444). A lesser mind can only deal with what is near it. The measures in Torah come "from the essence of wisdom itself" (line 445). A ruling of "kosher, permitted, pure" is the right hand that draws a thing close, so that "Godliness can dwell in it"; "forbidden" pushes it away (line 445). Every law holds both sides, fire and water, "from His right hand a fiery law" (line 446). Bring it home. When a law of Torah reaches into small, low details of your kitchen or your business, do not think it is beneath the Torah. The text says only the highest wisdom can bend that low. The picture of the sage breaks: a sage bends with effort. His wisdom bends without losing anything.

**From inside.** Respect for detailed laws. A sense that no corner of life is too low for Him. Some feel their plain, messy places become approachable.

**At the edge.** It points to "the primordial wisdom in the Essence itself" (line 444). The mind can learn a ruling; the Wisdom that reaches so low is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Keep one small, detailed law carefully today, and say: "Your wisdom bent down to here."

## Shaar Kriat Shema §98: a tree grows by water; limbs grow by the mind (lines 447-449)

**The movement.** It gives a picture of growth: a tree grows by water, a child's body grows only as long as his mind grows. So the mitzvot grow into all their details by the light of Torah. The mind is taught that learning is what makes the deeds grow.

> וכאשר יפסק גידול הרוחני יפסק גידול הגשמי כי הא בהא תליא
> *And when the spiritual growth stops, the physical growth stops, for one hangs on the other.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "a mitzvah is a lamp, Torah is light": the light of Torah is what makes the lamp of the mitzvah into a vessel (line 447). The 248 positive mitzvot are "the kindnesses that make it grow" (line 447). Step by step, the picture: a tree grows by sucking water through its roots; water spreads and makes more, fire contracts; a dry tree does not grow (line 447). A person is "a tree of the field": his body grows only while his mind and feelings grow, from six to twenty; "when the spiritual growth stops, the physical growth stops, for one hangs on the other" (line 448). "Days speak and many years teach wisdom", until seventy, "an elder who acquired wisdom" (line 448). So learning is the water that makes the mitzvot grow: the more Torah explaining how to do a mitzvah, the wider the vessel of the mitzvah becomes (line 449). "Study is greater because it leads to deed" means this: it grows the deed into all its details (line 449). Bring it home. If your mitzvot feel small and stunted, water them. Learn about one of them, and watch it grow wider in your hands. The picture breaks: a body stops growing at twenty. The text implies the mind, and so the mitzvot, can keep growing to seventy and beyond.

**From inside.** A sense of your spiritual life as something living and growing. Patience with slow growth. Some feel hungry to learn.

**At the edge.** It does not reach the edge here; it stays with growth and vessels.

**Into the day.** Learn one new detail about a mitzvah you already do, and do it today with that detail.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §99: "Be silent, so it rose in thought": a will with no reason (lines 450-452)

**The movement.** It finds the root of the mitzvot higher than the Torah: in a will with no reason at all. It separates a will with a hidden reason from a will that simply wants. The mind is brought before the Essence as pure wanting, beyond explanation.

> שתוק כך עלה במחשבה בלא טעם כלל וכלל
> *"Be silent; so it rose in thought", with no reason at all.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the root of the mitzvot is higher even than the root of the Torah: in the "skull", the crown above the brain, which covers the hidden wisdom (line 450). A person's skull is outside the brain and yet surrounds it; so the will surrounds the mind (line 450). Step by step: there are two kinds of will. One has a hidden reason: "I want this", and a reason lies concealed inside, which can later be found (line 451). The other has no reason at all: "I want it so", with no reason even hidden (line 451). When Moses asked about R. Akiva's end, he was told: "Be silent; so it rose in thought", with no reason at all (line 451). That will "above even a hidden reason" is "the crown of the crown", "counted as the Essence of the Emanator" (line 452). Inside it "the Essence of the Infinite rests" (line 452). The mitzvot, in their root, come from there. Bring it home. There are things you do only because He wants them, and you cannot explain them. The text says these touch the deepest place: not a reason, but His will itself. The picture of silence breaks: when we are told "be silent", it can feel like being shut out. Here the silence is the doorway to the Essence.

**From inside.** A quiet acceptance, without resentment. A strange closeness in not knowing why. Some feel their questions rest.

**At the edge.** Yes. The will without reason is "counted as the Essence of the Emanator" (line 452). The mind reaches for reasons; this is where it is told, gently, to stop.

**Into the day.** When you cannot understand something God asks, say: "Be silent; so it rose in thought," and do it anyway.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §100: learning the laws of sukkah draws the Essence into the mitzvah (lines 453-456)

**The movement.** It holds both sides, deed higher above and study higher below, and then asks why the Sages "decided". Its answer is surprising: study draws the Essence into the will of the mitzvah itself, because study is wisdom, and wisdom is "nothing", and only there does the true Nothing rest. The mind is given a reason to love learning the laws of what it does.

> כשלומד הל' סוכה ואתרוג מביא וממשיך עצמות אא"ס ברצון העליון הפשוט
> *When one learns the laws of sukkah and etrog, one brings and draws the Essence of the Infinite light into the simple higher will.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "these and those are the words of the living God": the deed is greater at its root, study is greater below (lines 453-454). But the Sages voted: study is greater. Why decide, if both are true (line 454)? Step by step: the soul is called "the thinking soul", though the mind is only a small part of it, because the whole soul dwells in its wisdom (line 455). A person begets "in his image" through his mind, not his will (line 455). So above: the Essence dwells most of all in wisdom, even more than in the simple will, "because the Essence of the Infinite is the true Nothing, and dwells only in nothing" (line 455). Therefore: "when one learns the laws of sukkah and etrog, one brings and draws the Essence of the Infinite light into the simple higher will" (line 455). Learning does not only teach how; it brings the Essence into the mitzvah's very root. "So Torah study is equal to them all" (line 456). Bring it home. Before a festival, before any mitzvah, learn its laws. The text says you are not only preparing; you are drawing the Essence into the mitzvah you will do. The picture of the thinking soul breaks: our minds are small. The text says that smallness, the "what?" of wisdom, is why the Essence can rest there.

**From inside.** Joy in learning halacha. Learning feels like a sacred act in itself. Some feel the mitzvah richer when they come to do it.

**At the edge.** Yes. The Essence "dwells only in nothing" (line 455). The mind can make itself "what?"; what rests in it is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Learn one law of the next mitzvah or festival you will do, and say: "I am drawing You into it."

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar Kriat Shema §101: the heart like a stone; before Him, darkness is like light (lines 457-459)

**The movement.** It asks what a sin's damage really is. The light is not lessened; the vessel gets a hole. The mind becomes "hard as a stone" from feeling itself too much. And the hardest thought: before His Essence, darkness and light are equal, so when light withdraws toward Him, even the outside can take it. The mind is sobered and humbled.

> והוא ע"י ענין טמטום המוח קשה כאבן בלי התפעלו' המוח
> *And it comes through the dullness of the mind, hard as stone, with no stirring of the mind.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: why did God forgive idolatry in the First Temple but not neglect of Torah in the Second (line 457)? First: what is the damage of a sin? The light never lessens. The vessel is holed, like a cup that leaks (line 457). The 248 mitzvot are vessels for the light of the Name in each mitzvah (line 457). Step by step: the yud of the Name, wisdom, is damaged in the soul by "the dullness of the mind, hard as stone, with no stirring", from feeling oneself too much, too heavy to move, like a stone (line 458). The heh, understanding, is damaged when one understands only plain words, with no feeling for God (line 458). How can holy light leak to the outside at all? After the tzimtzum everything, even the border of holiness, is measured (line 459). But "before Him, darkness is truly like light; both are equal" (line 459). When light withdraws toward the Essence, all can receive it, because nothing takes up place before Him (line 459). That is also why the exile is long. Bring it home. Notice when your mind feels heavy as a stone in prayer. The text names the cause: too much feeling of yourself. The cure is not force; it is setting yourself aside a little. The picture of the stone breaks: a stone cannot choose to soften. A person can, by stepping back from himself.

**From inside.** Honest recognition of heaviness, without despair. A sober awe at how far His Essence is beyond our categories. Some feel the stone begin to warm.

**At the edge.** Yes. "Before Him, darkness is like light" (line 459). The mind needs the difference; before His Essence it is not there.

**Into the day.** When you notice your heart feels like stone today, set aside one thing you want for yourself, and see if it softens.

## Shaar Kriat Shema §102: Torah learners draw new light from the Essence; the deed in the time to come (lines 460-464)

**The movement.** It finishes the Gate of the Shema. Those who learned Torah drew new light from the very Essence to fill every gap; without Torah there was no remedy. Then it looks ahead: when all the sparks are refined, the deed will be greater, and the Essence will be revealed below as above. The mind ends the long gate between present learning and future revealing.

> ועוד דע"י לומדי התורה הי' נמשך גלוי אור חדש מעצמו' המאציל לגמרי
> *And furthermore, through those who learned Torah, a new light was drawn from the very Essence of the Emanator.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: afflictions (nega) are the opposite of delight (oneg) (line 461). A person whose image of God is damaged has no bitul; "its sign is that it has no bitul in its place, but is hard as stone" (line 461). Step by step: in the First Temple they learned Torah, and "through those who learned Torah a new light was drawn from the very Essence of the Emanator", for the Infinite rests in wisdom; that new light filled every lack left by sins (line 462). In the Second, they left the Torah, and "the land was lost", because nothing else can fill the lack (line 462). But now wisdom sorts; later, when all the sparks are refined, the deed will be greater than study (line 463). "On that day living waters will flow from Jerusalem" (line 463). Then the whole Essence in His inner will, now only in hidden paths, will be revealed, "below as above, equally" (line 464). Then the mitzvot "are the Essence itself, not only a vessel" (line 464). Bring it home. Your learning today draws new light into whatever is lacking, in you and in the world. And your deeds are storing up a revealing still to come. The picture of filling a lack breaks: some losses in this world are not filled. The text trusts that the new light is from the Essence, which is not limited by what was lost.

**From inside.** Hope and responsibility. A reason to learn even when tired. Some feel they are part of a long story that ends in light.

**At the edge.** Yes. The new light is "from the very Essence of the Emanator" (line 462), and in the end the mitzvot will be "the Essence itself" (line 464). The mind can learn and do; the revealing is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Learn a few lines of Torah today for the sake of whatever feels broken, and trust it draws new light.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §1: "He wraps Himself in light as a garment" (lines 467-470)

**The movement.** It opens the Gate of Tzitzit with the tallit as God's garment of light. A garment hides and also lets light through, as a thought clothes a deep idea so the mind can hold it. The mind is taught to see the covering as the very thing that lets it receive.

> אך הוצרך להלביש את אור העצמות בלבוש
> *But it was necessary to clothe the light of the Essence in a garment.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Make yourselves tzitzit... and you shall see it and remember all the mitzvot... and not stray after your heart", and then again, "so that you remember and do... and be holy" (line 467). Why two rememberings? The gate starts from "Let us make man in our image": how can the finite be an image of the Infinite (lines 467-468)? Step by step: "He wraps Himself in light as a garment" (line 468). A garment covers the light so it does not shine as it is; yet light breaks through the weave of the cloth (line 468). The human picture: a deep idea cannot be held by the mind until it is clothed in the letters of thought. Without that garment the mind is confused; so the garment of thought has an advantage over the bare idea (line 469). So above: "it was necessary to clothe the light of the Essence in a garment" (line 469). That light is the Torah, and its outer, revealed layer clothes the inner Infinite light, so that it shines below in the 248 mitzvot, in measure (line 470). Bring it home. When you put on a tallit, or any garment of a mitzvah, you are wrapped in something that both hides and carries His light. The covering is how it reaches you. The picture of cloth breaks: cloth only blocks. This garment is woven so that the light breaks through.

**From inside.** Being wrapped. Safety and nearness together. Some feel held rather than hidden from.

**At the edge.** Yes. What is clothed is "the light of the Essence", "simple light beyond compare" (line 469). The mind holds the garment; the light inside is beyond it.

**Into the day.** When you wrap yourself in a tallit, or dress in the morning, say: "You wrap Yourself in light as a garment."

## Shaar HaTzitzit §2: Israel join the hidden Torah to the revealed (lines 471-473)

**The movement.** It says only Israel can join the inner Torah to the outer, because Israel rose in His thought from His innermost Essence. Then it shows the tallit as the surrounding light that comes to Israel from His very power. The mind is shown its own role as the joiner.

> לפי שישראל עלו במח' ג"כ בבחי' הפנימית והעצמות דאא"ס
> *Because Israel rose in thought also in the innermost and the Essence of the Infinite light.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "The hidden things belong to God, and the revealed to us and our children, to do all the words of this Torah" (line 471). No one can join the Infinite in the inner Torah to its outer garments in measure, except Israel, "because Israel rose in thought also in the innermost and the Essence of the Infinite light" (line 471). Step by step: "You are beautiful, my love, you are beautiful": twice, once from below and once from above (line 471). The four faces of the chariot, lion, ox, eagle and man, are four ways the "something" rises to the "nothing"; the face of man includes them all, through the bitul of wisdom (lines 471-472). "May God make His face shine to you" comes down in the same four colors (line 472). The tallit is the King's spread-out garment, "He wraps Himself in light" (line 473). These flows reach Israel only as surrounding light, because they come from the power of the Infinite itself (line 473). Bring it home. When you learn a plain law and do it, you are joining His hidden self to the revealed world. That is why it was given to you. The picture of the chariot breaks: a chariot is ridden. Here those who carry Him are also the ones who join Him to the world.

**From inside.** A sense of purpose. A quiet dignity in being one who joins. Some feel their Jewishness as something deeper than identity.

**At the edge.** Yes. Israel's root is in "the Essence of the Infinite light" (line 471). The mind can act on it; the root is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Before you do a mitzvah today, say: "I am joining Your hidden self to this."

## Shaar HaTzitzit §3: the blue thread that burns, and the white that turns (lines 474-478)

**The movement.** It separates two ways of dealing with evil. The techelet, blue, is a consuming fire: anger at the evil inclination drives it out. The white threads come from a place where evil is not even known, and they turn darkness into light by themselves. The mind is shown both the fight and the transformation.

> אתהפכא חשוכא לנהורא ממילא ומאיליו
> *Darkness is turned to light on its own, by itself.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the techelet is Malchut, "a fire that eats and consumes" (line 475). It is the surrounding light the outside forces flee from; one careful with tzitzit had them slap his face to stop him from sinning (line 475). Its way is severity: a person in whom God's light shines will be angry at his evil inclination and cannot bear it, like a father who rebukes his son; "you shall burn the evil from your midst" (line 476). Step by step: the white threads come from the tree of life, above good and evil, where the nature of evil is not known at all (line 477). They do not fight evil; through them "darkness is turned to light on its own, by itself" (line 476). And a delicate point: the more a person recognizes his Creator, the more he feels even the finest evil in himself (line 477). "You shall not stray after your heart" is then not a strain but something that follows by itself (line 478). Bring it home. There are times to be angry at what is bad in you and push it out. And there are times to simply bring in more light, and let the darkness turn. Know which time it is. The picture of fire breaks: fire destroys what it touches. The white light does not destroy; it changes.

**From inside.** Clear strength against a bad habit. And a softer trust that more good will change you. Some feel both at once.

**At the edge.** It points to the white threads' root "above the tree of good and evil" (line 477). The mind can fight; the turning of darkness to light comes from beyond it.

**Into the day.** Today, instead of fighting one small bad habit, add one good act in the same place, and watch it turn.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §4: "I am first and I am last"; the will in every limb (lines 479-481)

**The movement.** It asks how the Essence, which no one knows, can say "I". The crown stands between: its hidden side is "Ayin", nothing, and its revealed side is "Ani", I. The picture is the will, which rests equally in every limb. The mind learns to find the same "I" at the beginning and at the end.

> ואני אחרון אחר האצי' הכל בהשוואה א'
> *"And I am last", after Atzilut: all in one equality.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: a person says "I" when everyone knows who he is; "I am the one who did it" (line 479). But the Essence is beyond all knowing; how can "I" be said of Him (line 479)? The crown is the middle: its inner side, known to no one, is "Ayin"; its side that is a source is "Ani", the same letters turned around (line 480). Step by step, the soul's picture: the will moves the body even without our seeing; Daniel's friends trembled though they saw nothing, because "their mazal saw" (line 481). A sudden thought of teshuvah stirs the body with regret before one knows why (line 481). The will rests in every limb, great and small, and in opposites, all equally (line 480). So "I am first", in wisdom at the head, "and I am last", after Atzilut: "all in one equality" (line 481). The yechidah, the soul's deepest point, always hovers hidden, "surely man walks in an image" (line 481). Bring it home. The same "I" that began your life is present at its plainest moments. Say "I" today and think: there is a deeper "I" in me, and a deeper "I" above, the same at every step. The picture of the will breaks: our will changes. The "I" above is equal first and last.

**From inside.** A sense of continuity. The ordinary end of the day is not further from Him than its holy start. Some feel their own "I" as a small echo of His.

**At the edge.** Yes. The inner crown is "Ayin", "counted as the last level of the Essence" (line 480). The mind can say "I"; the Nothing behind it is beyond.

**Into the day.** At the end of the day, say: "I am first and I am last," and think of Him as equally present in the evening as in the morning.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §5: Israel make Him "I" and make Him holy; joy with trembling (lines 482-487)

**The movement.** It gives one of the book's boldest thoughts: in Himself He cannot be called holy or hidden, since there is only He and His Name. Only when He consulted the souls of Israel did "Nothing" become "I". Israel draw Him into being "I" and "Holy". Then it joins joy and trembling, and names the higher fear as bitul. The mind is shown how much depends on it.

> וא"כ ישראל הם המשיכי' גם לאני
> *So it is Israel who draw down even the "I".*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "I am God": "I am the one who lowered Myself to be called 'I'" (line 482). Who caused "Nothing" to become "I"? Israel, who rose in His thought: "With whom did He consult? With the souls of the righteous" (line 482). "So it is Israel who draw down even the 'I'" (line 482). And "from whom is He hidden, when there is only He and His Name?" (line 482). Step by step: "you shall be holy, for I am holy": through the mitzvot Israel become holy with the holiness of the Essence itself (line 483). "My mitzvot" means "tzavta", joining: the Essence joined with Israel (line 483). Joy and fear are opposites, yet "rejoice with trembling": in the place of joy there is trembling (line 484). When evil is removed there is song, and it reaches His place, where there is no evil at all (line 484). The 32 threads are the bitul of "what?" (line 485). "If there is no wisdom there is no fear": the higher fear is wisdom itself, "bitul in essence", like one who enters the king's palace and falls into bitul (line 487). Godly wisdom does not dwell in the proud, who make it a "something" for themselves (line 487). Bring it home. Your mitzvah today is not only obedience. It is part of how He becomes "I" to the world, and holy in it. Carry that with joy and trembling together. The picture of consulting breaks: a king consults because he needs advice. He "consulted" out of desire, not need.

**From inside.** Awe at your own importance, without pride. Joy and trembling in one breath. Some feel a new seriousness, gentle and glad.

**At the edge.** Yes. In Himself, "only He and His Name", nothing can even be called hidden or holy (line 482). The mind stands where words like "holy" start; before that is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Before one mitzvah today, say: "Through this You are holy in the world," and do it with joy and a little trembling.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §7: sparks rise by bitul; the face that shines on the small (lines 488-491)

**The movement.** It follows the fallen sparks: when God's light is revealed beyond the usual measure, the shells dissolve and the good in them rises, by bitul, into the Shechinah. Then it pictures a great one looking at a small one until his face shines. The mind learns that it rises by being included, and shines by being looked at.

> שמתבררי' ועולין בבחי' בטול והתכללות בשכינ'
> *They are sorted and rise through bitul and being included in the Shechinah.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the techelet is "black fire", consuming evil; the white threads are "white fire" (line 488). Malchut goes down into the lowest halls to sort the sparks (line 488). When Godly light is revealed there more than the hiding allows, the shells are nullified, and the good in them is included in holiness (line 488). Step by step, the picture: Abraham, whom the people of Chet called "a prince of God among us", and many converts joined him (line 489). So the fallen sparks "are sorted and rise through bitul and being included in the Shechinah" (line 489). The great beast that eats a thousand mountains each day: the good sorted daily and absorbed (line 489). And beauty comes from above: "as a great one looks at a small one, strengthening him until his face shines"; from the light of a teacher's face, the student's face shines in halacha (line 490). "May God make His face shine to you" (line 490). The first garment, between the Emanator and the emanated, is all bright light (line 490). Bring it home. You rise not by becoming bigger but by being included. And you shine not by your own effort alone but by being seen by Him. Let yourself be looked at in prayer. The picture of the teacher breaks: a teacher's gaze can turn away. "The eyes of all look to You" (line 490), and His do not turn away.

**From inside.** The warmth of being seen. A lightness in giving yourself over. Some feel their face relax and brighten after prayer.

**At the edge.** It points to the garment "between the Emanator and the emanated", "all bright light" (line 490). The mind feels the face shining; the Face is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Spend a moment today with a teacher or an elder, simply receiving their attention.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §8: the hidden "I" is in everything equally because it is hidden from everything (lines 492-494)

**The movement.** It explains why the hidden "I" of the crown fills heaven and earth equally. Because it is hidden from everything equally, it can clothe itself in everything equally. And it comes into the Name only through the deed of the mitzvot. The mind learns that hiddenness is the condition of presence.

> משום שנסתר מהכל בשוה ע"כ התלבשותו בהעלם בכולם בשוה
> *Because it is hidden from everything equally, therefore its clothing in hiding is in all of them equally.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "the mitzvot of God" are the King's revealed commands in the world of making; "My mitzvot" are the Essence's own will (line 492). A person's nature from childhood stays the same into old age; only its control changes (line 493). So the will and delight above the mind do not change from small to great (line 493). "Surely man walks in an image": the surrounding lights add revelation from hiding (line 493). Step by step: "I" has two meanings. One is "I" spoken to another: "I command you" (line 494). The other is "I am who I am", said of oneself in hiding, unknown (line 494). The hidden "I" is in everything equally, "because it is hidden from everything equally, therefore its clothing in hiding is in all of them equally" (line 494). So "I am first and I am last", "see that I, I am He", "because their beginning is wedged in their end" (line 494). And this hidden "I" comes into the Name "only through the deed of the mitzvot" (line 494). Bring it home. You may wish He were more obvious. The text says it is exactly because He is hidden from all that He can be in all, equally, including you now. The picture of "I am who I am" breaks: a person who hides is avoiding something. His hiding is a way of being everywhere.

**From inside.** A calm certainty that He is here, not in spite of the hiding but through it. Some feel less need to find signs.

**At the edge.** Yes. "I am who I am", "unknown at all" (line 494). The mind cannot find Him as an object; it can know He is equally in all.

**Into the day.** Look at the most ordinary object near you and say: "Hidden from all equally, in all equally."

**Ladder.** L04 (Held from inside and all around)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §9: first the blue, then the white; what comes lowest has the highest root (lines 495-499)

**The movement.** It orders the verses: first seeing the techelet and its bitul, then the joy of "My mitzvot". It praises what falls lowest as rooted highest, and says the hidden and the revealed Torah are one Torah. Then it begins again from "in our image". The mind is taught to honor the lowest things.

> דמה שבא למטה מטה ביותר מזה מוכח ששרשו הגבה למעלה יותר
> *What comes down lowest of all shows that its root is higher.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: first "you shall see it": the techelet, the yoke of Heaven, the lower fear, bitul of the self (line 495). Then "you shall remember all the mitzvot of God", the higher mitzvot (line 495). Then the second remembering, higher still, to draw from the Essence of the Emanator; "and you shall be holy" (lines 495-496). Removing evil is purity, and purity leads to holiness (line 496). Step by step: Malchut is called "the fallen fruit of the higher wisdom", which sounds like a lowering (line 497). But it is praise: her counted letters renew creation every day without end (line 497). "What comes down lowest of all shows that its root is higher" (line 497). And the inner Torah and the revealed Torah are "one, not two Torahs" (line 497); both come from the Essence (line 498). The eagle's mercy carries its young on its wings: mercy that reaches from the highest to the lowest pit (line 498). And the gate asks again why only man is "in our image" (line 499). Bring it home. When you meet something very low, a hard person, a plain task, a coarse part of yourself, remember the rule: what comes lowest may have the highest root. Treat it with care. The picture of fallen fruit breaks: fallen fruit rots. This fallen fruit renews the world every day.

**From inside.** Reverence for low things and people. Less contempt, more curiosity. Some feel their own low places differently.

**At the edge.** It points to the Essence, from which inner and outer Torah come as one (line 498). The mind sees the lowest; the highest root is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Treat the lowest task you have today as something with a high root, and do it carefully.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §11: the thought that hides and reveals; light out of the hiding (lines 500-501)

**The movement.** It asks why an idea can only come into the heart through the letters of thought, when thought itself wanders among trivial things. Then it says the same about God's hiding: the hiding holds two opposites, to conceal and to be the source of revealing. The mind learns to trust that light breaks out of the dark that covers it.

> ונמצא יש בו ב' הפכים להסתיר ולהעלים ממש ולהיות מזה מקור לגלוי אור
> *So it holds two opposites: truly to hide and conceal, and from this to be the source of revealed light.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the real thing is the light of the idea itself; thought, by nature, is far from it, wandering even among the simplest matters, sometimes only in imagination (line 500). Yet no idea can come into the heart except by clothing itself in the letters of thought (line 500). Step by step: above, the Essence is utterly beyond, beyond even the first thought (line 501). "He made darkness His hiding place": this too was a contraction of His very light, "You are a God who hides Himself" (line 501). And from that hiding and concealing the light breaks out: "He reveals deep things out of darkness" (line 501). "So it holds two opposites: truly to hide and conceal, and from this to be the source of revealed light" (line 501). The hiding is not an obstacle to the light. It is where the light comes from.
