Bring it home. When your own thoughts feel scattered and ordinary, remember: these same letters of thought are the only way a true insight can reach you. Do not despise them; give them something true to hold. The picture of wandering thought breaks: our wandering is a weakness. His hiding is chosen, and is already a source.

**From inside.** Patience with a busy mind. Hope in dark times. Some feel the dark itself as full, not empty.

**At the edge.** Yes. "The Essence of the Infinite light is utterly beyond", with no comparison even to the first thought (line 501). The mind can hold letters; the Light behind the hiding is beyond it.

**Into the day.** When your mind wanders in prayer today, gently give it the words of the prayer to hold, without anger.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §12: the bright garment; black fire on white fire; the Essence in measure (lines 502-505)

**The movement.** It reads "He wraps Himself in light" the other way: not hiding, but light spread out as a bright white garment. Both readings are true. The inner Torah is white fire, the revealed Torah black fire on it, and Israel join them. The Essence dwells in measure and in the lowest level as above. The mind is shown the Holy of Holies in a small, measured room.

> גם בגבול ושעור ובתחתי' המדרגה הוא שורה כמו למעלה ממש
> *Even in limit and measure, and at the lowest level, He dwells just as above.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "He wraps Himself in light": the plain meaning is not that the light is darkened by the garment, but that the light itself becomes a garment, "His clothing white as snow" (line 502). R. Meir's Torah had "garments of light" with an aleph: a bright, pure garment (line 502). The text says both readings are true (lines 501-502). Step by step: "the hidden things belong to God": the secrets and reasons of Torah, hidden in the Essence; "the revealed belong to us": the Torah that comes into deeds (line 503). "From His right hand a fiery law": the Torah was given as black fire on white fire. The white fire is the inner Torah, without limit; the black fire is the outer Torah, that separates pure from impure (lines 503-504). The inner Torah gives the outer its strength to sort good from evil (line 504). And the Essence "even in limit and measure, and at the lowest level, dwells just as above" (line 504). The proof: "the heavens cannot contain You", and yet He contracted His presence into the Holy of Holies, a room with measurements (line 505). Bring it home. When you look at the black letters of a Torah scroll, remember the white parchment around them: the white fire, without limit, and the black fire, measured, both one Torah. Your measured life can hold Him too. The picture of the Holy of Holies breaks for us: we have no Temple now. The text's point remains: He can dwell in measure.

**From inside.** Reverence for measured, detailed life. Wonder at black on white. Some feel their small spaces become holy.

**At the edge.** Yes. The inner Torah shines "from the light of the Essence of the Infinite itself" (line 503). The mind reads the black letters; the white fire is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Look at a page of Torah today and notice both the letters and the white space, and say: "Black fire on white fire."

## Shaar HaTzitzit §13: the tallit and the four faces; mercy that joins the head to the heels (lines 506-509)

**The movement.** It explains why tzitzit is weighed against all the mitzvot: the tallit is the surrounding light, and its threads break out from it. Then it ranks the four faces of the chariot and shows great mercy joining the highest to the lowest, even the "heels", the last and lowest generation. The mind is included in a mercy that reaches down to it.

> וגם לקטני הערך נשמות היותר שפלים כדור האחרון הזה שנק' עקביים
> *And also to the souls of small worth, the lowest, like this last generation, which is called the heels.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "you shall see it and remember all the mitzvot": tzitzit equals all the mitzvot, because the tallit is the higher surrounding light in the inner Infinite (line 506). As a person wraps himself in his tallit, head and body covered, white and clean, so "His clothing is white as snow" (line 506). The 32 threads are lights breaking out of it (line 506). Step by step: the face of man includes all: "a man is like the One above" (line 507). A lion's great love from below wakes great love above; an ox's fear wakes fear above; and the eagle's mercy wakes the higher mercy above both (line 508). One who feels great mercy for his own divine soul, in great bitterness, draws down the higher mercy (line 509). That mercy joins the highest end, the inner Torah, "longer than the earth", to the lowest end, physical deeds, "and also to the souls of small worth, the lowest, like this last generation, which is called the heels" (line 509). "You are all beautiful, my love, from head to foot" (line 509). Bring it home. If you feel you belong to a small, low generation, the text says mercy is exactly what reaches the heels. Have mercy on your own soul, and it draws the higher mercy. The picture of heels breaks: heels are the least sensitive part. The text says they, too, are "all beautiful".

**From inside.** Compassion for your own soul, not self-pity. A feeling of being included from head to foot. Some feel tender toward their whole generation.

**At the edge.** It points to the higher mercy "above both" love and fear (line 508). The mind can feel mercy; the Mercy that answers is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Think of the part of you that feels furthest from God, and feel mercy for it, not blame.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §14: the corners of the garment; the yechidah is the power of the Essence in the soul (lines 510-512)

**The movement.** It explains why the threads are on the corners: the Essence as it is can only shine as surrounding light, and its lights break out at the edges. Then it names the soul's deepest point, the yechidah, as the power of the Essence itself in a person, and separates the part of the soul that is above evil from the part that must choose. The mind is shown where it is untouched.

> שבחי' יחידה שבנפש הוא כח העצמות שבנפש ממש
> *For the yechidah of the soul is the very power of the Essence in the soul.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "on the corners of their garments" (line 510). The true shining of the Essence "cannot shine except as the higher surrounding light"; so He wraps Himself in a tallit over the head (line 510). "The yechidah of the soul is the very power of the Essence in the soul" (line 510). Step by step: on the right corner the surrounding kindness shines, "His right hand embraces me", like one who hugs his friend (line 511). The higher love wraps the soul when it wraps itself in the tallit, guarding it all day and stirring it to every mitzvah (line 511). On the left, awe (line 511). There are two levels in the soul. Its essential part, "cleaving above", has no evil at all: "you are all beautiful, there is no blemish in you"; from there comes a light that despises evil without force (line 512). But the part in the body, clothed in good and evil, must choose, sometimes good and sometimes the opposite: "choose life" (line 512). Bring it home. There is a part of you that has never been touched by any wrong you did. Wrap yourself in that knowledge, like a tallit, and then make today's choices from there. The picture of the hug breaks: a friend's hug ends. This embrace surrounds all day.

**From inside.** A sense of an untouched core. Relief from total self-blame. A strength to choose, coming from inside.

**At the edge.** Yes. The yechidah is "the very power of the Essence in the soul" (line 510). The mind can know it is there; it cannot see it.

**Into the day.** When you put on a tallit or a garment today, say: "Your right hand embraces me," and feel wrapped.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §15: kindness that sweetens, and teshuvah that makes evil itself good (lines 513-515)

**The movement.** It holds two ways of transformation. The white threads, kindness, overwhelm the harsh judgments until evil has no place. The techelet, teshuvah, does something truer: it makes the evil itself into good. The one who returns stands higher than the perfectly righteous. The mind is given the dignity of teshuvah.

> וזה כח התשוב' אמיתי' שעוש' מן הרע עצמו טוב
> *And this is the power of true teshuvah, which makes good out of evil itself.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "you shall see it and remember... and not stray": this is choosing, "turning away from evil", subduing, "the foundation of the whole Torah" (line 513). The whole of Torah and mitzvot is "to turn darkness into light" (line 513). Step by step: kindness from above sweetens the harsh judgments, "His kindness overcame", "Your right hand, God, shatters the enemy"; a person feels this on Shabbat, delighting in God (line 514). But "this is the power of true teshuvah, which makes good out of evil itself" (line 514). Like fire that eats fire: the great bitterness of one who returns removes evil from its root (line 515). "One may acquire his world in one hour": the one who returns is greater than the perfectly righteous (line 515). This is purity that brings to holiness (line 515). The rising light goes above the descending light (line 515). Bring it home. If you have done wrong, the text does not ask you only to cover it with good deeds. It says true teshuvah can turn the wrong itself into something good. That is its power. The picture of fire eating fire breaks: fire leaves ash. Teshuvah leaves something good.

**From inside.** A clean, serious hope. The past becomes material, not only a burden. Some feel a quiet courage to face what they did.

**At the edge.** It points to a turning that reaches the root of evil itself (line 515). The mind can regret; the turning of evil into good comes from beyond it.

**Into the day.** Choose one thing you regret, and do one act today that turns its energy toward good.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §16: the primordial air where darkness and light are equal; the lamp and the flame (lines 516-518)

**The movement.** It roots the power of teshuvah in something above light itself, a hidden "air" where darkness and light are the same. Only a fire from there can make darkness shine. Then it separates the soul as a lamp, which pushes darkness away for a time, from the soul as "the flame of Yah", which transforms, and warns against mistaking natural heat for it. The mind is taught to seek the real flame.

> גם חשך לא יחשיך כחשיכה כאורה שוין שם
> *"Even darkness does not darken"; darkness and light are equal there.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: if both kindness and teshuvah turn evil to good, what is the difference (line 516)? The techelet is "a fire that eats fire", and its root is higher than light itself (line 516). Above light there is a hidden primordial "air"; there "even darkness does not darken; darkness and light are equal there" (line 516). In the future "night will shine like day" (line 517). Step by step: "the lamp of God is the soul of man" (line 517). When the soul praises God with great love, as on Shabbat, it gets strength to light even the darkness of the animal soul, to despise evil (line 517). That is the lamp: it pushes darkness away, but only for a time (line 517). But the soul as "the flame of Yah" eats the alien fire until the person "becomes another man" (line 517). "Many err in this", taking natural heat for this flame (line 517). The one who returns is higher than the righteous, "because even the darkness itself shines" (line 518). And the lamp of the mitzvah is rooted higher than the light of Torah (line 518). Bring it home. Notice the difference between feeling warm in prayer and truly changing. The text honors the warmth, but warns: the real flame is the one that makes you another person. The picture of fire breaks: fire is light. The root the text points to is above light, where darkness is not an enemy.

**From inside.** Humility about your own fervor. A longing for real change, not only feeling. Some feel a sober hope.

**At the edge.** Yes. The root is where "darkness and light are equal" (line 516). The mind knows light against dark; this is beyond it.

**Into the day.** After a strong feeling in prayer today, ask: "What will I do differently because of this?" and do it.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §17: "Do not stray after your hearts": one heart for God alone (lines 519-521)

**The movement.** It reads "you shall not stray" as the loyalty of a bride: Israel receive only from their Beloved, and the heart is not divided. Even busy with livelihood, the heart can be one, trusting in Him. The mind is taught faithfulness as a simple inner stance.

> לב' א' יהי' לה' לבד ואין זר אתו
> *The heart shall be one, for God alone, with no stranger with Him.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "you shall see it": the techelet, "a consuming fire"; like one who sees the king in his greatness and falls into awe, accepting his rule and no other (line 519). "You shall not stray after your heart and your eyes, after which you go astray" (line 520). Step by step, the text's picture: a wife who seeks pleasure and bond from others besides her husband is called unfaithful, even if she does not leave him (line 520). So Israel, the bride, receive God's light in mind and heart, and "one heart to their Father in heaven": "whom do I have in heaven but You?" (line 520). Even in exile, some serve God and also other powers (line 521). But "the heart shall be one, for God alone, with no stranger with Him" (line 521). Even when busy with business and many thoughts not about God, he trusts in God and casts his burden on Him (line 521). When a bad thought falls in, he pushes it away, is pained by it, and returns (line 521). Bring it home. You will think many things today that are not about God. That is not straying. Straying is placing your trust or your delight elsewhere. Keep the heart one. The picture of marriage breaks: a spouse can be jealous for wrong reasons. His "jealousy" is for your own wholeness.

**From inside.** Simplicity. A single center under all the busy thoughts. Some feel relief from inner division.

**At the edge.** It does not reach the edge here; it stays with the heart's loyalty.

**Into the day.** When you worry about money today, say: "My heart is one, for You alone; I cast my burden on You."

**Ladder.** L07 (Know it today, bring it to the heart)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §18: "I am God": only self-giving reaches the Essence (lines 522-525)

**The movement.** It asks again what "I am God" means, when nothing knows Him, and says what we know is "a glimmer of a glimmer". Keter is both "I" and "Nothing". The Essence itself is not reached by the will of delight but only by giving the soul's very point. The mind is shown what reaches where.

> בסדכ"ס עצמו גם זה הרצון לא יגיע כ"א במס"נ בכל עצם נקודת הנפש
> *In the hidden of all hidden itself, even this will does not reach, except by self-giving with the whole essential point of the soul.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "I am God": a person says "I" when he is known; but the Essence is known to no one (line 522). What is known in any world "is not His essence, only a glimmer of a glimmer" (line 522). Keter is both "Ani" and "Ayin" (line 523). Step by step, the soul's picture: will and delight are not held in any one organ, and yet the whole body moves by them equally; "I fill heaven and earth equally": the general surrounding light (line 523). Prayers in the Amidah, "may it be Your will", reach the sefirot (line 524). But "in the hidden of all hidden itself, even this will does not reach, except by self-giving with the whole essential point of the soul", above even the will to delight in God (line 524). That is the essential will, bitul of will, like the one who returns (line 524). Then the Essence dwells in the mitzvot (line 524). "In the place of His greatness, great humility" (line 525). Bring it home. Even the will to enjoy God is still your will. The text asks for something simpler: to give your whole self, not for a feeling, just because He is. The picture of the body moving by will breaks: a body can refuse. The self-giving here is the soul's own deepest yes.

**From inside.** A stillness beyond wanting. Some feel a simple readiness, with no need to get anything back.

**At the edge.** Yes. Only self-giving reaches "the hidden of all hidden" (line 524). The mind cannot go there; the point of the soul can.

**Into the day.** In the Shema today, at "with all your soul", say it as a real giving, wanting nothing in return.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §19: "Holy" cannot be said of the One who is alone; the light stored in the deed (lines 526-528)

**The movement.** It explains holiness: "holy" means set apart from something lesser, and cannot be said of the Essence alone, who has no other. So the holiness of the mitzvot is only a tip of His holiness now, and its full light is stored inside the physical deed for the future. The mind learns that its plain deeds hold a hidden treasure.

> אבל כשהוא יחידי ואין לו חבר להתייחס עמו
> *But when He is alone, and has no companion to be related with.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: first purity, "you shall not stray", then holiness, "you shall be holy" (line 526). Purity brings to holiness (line 526). "Holy" means separate from something lower. "But when He is alone, and has no companion to be related with", even "holy" does not apply (line 526). Step by step: "My holiness is above yours" (line 527). The holiness of the mitzvot now is only "the tip of a glimmer" of the holiness of His Essence (line 527). "There is none holy like God": the Torah is holy, Israel are holy, but none like Him (line 527). Yet "by Your light we shall see light" in the future: the great light of His holiness is "stored and clothed in the final deed, in the mitzvah of now", because "their beginning is wedged in their end" (line 528). "The end of the matter: fear God and keep His mitzvot, for this is all of man" (line 528). Bring it home. The mitzvah you do today may feel small. The text says the whole light of His holiness is stored inside exactly that deed, waiting. The picture of storage breaks: stored things can be lost. This light is kept by Him.

**From inside.** A sense of treasure in plain acts. Patience for the future. Some feel their deeds become heavy with meaning.

**At the edge.** Yes. He is "alone", with no one to be related to; "holy" is already a word from our side (line 526). The mind can say "holy"; the One alone is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Do one mitzvah today slowly, knowing the light of His holiness is stored in it.

## Shaar HaTzitzit §20: white and blue; death itself becomes life (lines 529-531)

**The movement.** It reads the white and the blue as light coming down and light going up. The rising light, teshuvah, reaches higher: it is like death itself becoming life, higher than life. Then it says that one who comes to purify himself is helped from above. The mind is given the resurrection as a picture of return.

> שזהו כמו שהמות עצמו נעשה חיים שהוא יותר נעלה מגוף אור החיים
> *For this is like death itself becoming life, which is higher than the light of life itself.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "so that you remember and do all My mitzvot": the second remembering, the holiness of the Essence (line 529). How can purity, only avoiding evil, bring to such holiness (line 529)? White threads are light from above, lighting the darkness down to the lowest depths; the techelet is the yoke of Heaven from below (line 530). Step by step: the Essence's holiness is reached only by the teshuvah that turns evil itself to good, "for this is like death itself becoming life, which is higher than the light of life itself" (line 530). That is the root of the resurrection (line 530). "Earth came before heaven": the end of the deed rose first in thought (line 530). "One who comes to purify himself is helped from above": "the Lord is with my helpers" (line 531). The hidden darkness reveals the Essence more than the garment of light (line 531). Joy and fear join: in the place of the joy of return there must be trembling (line 531). Bring it home. If some part of you feels dead, spiritually, the text does not say give up on it. It says that part, turned, can become more alive than what never died. The picture of resurrection breaks for now: we do not see it. The text holds it as a promise and as a pattern for teshuvah.

**From inside.** Hope for what seemed lost. The sense of help coming as soon as you begin. Some feel joy with a tremble.

**At the edge.** Yes. The hidden darkness "will light the light of the Essence itself" (line 531). The mind can begin to return; the life it finds is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Take the first small step in some return you have been putting off, and trust that help will come.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §21: Korach and Aharon; the foot that moves at once; love that wakes sudden teshuvah (lines 532-542)

**The movement.** It reads Korach's fight as a fight about kindness and severity. Aharon's great love, above reason, wakes sudden teshuvah even in those deep in sin. Korach wanted severity separate from kindness. The will is present in the foot as in the brain; so He is in all equally. And even Sheol is not wholly apart from Him, though sin itself is worse than Sheol. The mind is given love as the power that wakes return.

> יעוררם ברוח התעוררות תשובה פתאום בלתי הכנה כלל מקודם
> *It wakes them with a spirit of sudden teshuvah, with no preparation at all beforehand.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: Korach brought his people in tallitot all of blue, and mocked: if one blue thread frees a garment, does a garment all of blue not free itself (line 533)? The text reads it: the jealousy was over Aharon, the priest of kindness (lines 532-533). The will is present equally in every limb: the foot moves the instant one wills, though a wound in the foot does not disturb thought (line 534). So He watches over the end of the earth as over the beginning (line 534). Step by step: Aharon was anointed with oil on his head, the source of great love above reason (line 540). "With great love You have loved us" (line 538). That love can wake love in the hearts of Israel below, even when they have turned away, busy with livelihood, even in their sins: "it wakes them with a spirit of sudden teshuvah, with no preparation at all beforehand" (line 538). "Return, rebellious children" (line 538). Korach wanted severity separate from kindness, not included in it (line 540). Even Sheol is not wholly separate from Godliness (line 541). And yet "the sin itself is worse than Sheol", for it is "separated in essence entirely from His unity" (line 542). The text holds both. Bring it home. When someone you know seems far away, remember Aharon: great love, above reason, can wake a sudden return. Give love, not only rebuke. The picture of the foot breaks: our will can be blocked by injury. His presence in the lowest is not blocked.

**From inside.** Warmth toward those who are far. A hope for sudden change, in others and yourself. Some feel a pull to be gentle.

**At the edge.** It points to love "above reason and mind entirely" (line 538). The mind can give love; the Love that wakes return is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Speak one warm word today to someone who seems far from Torah, without any rebuke.

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTzitzit §23: the prohibitions are rooted higher; the lower the fall, the higher the rise (lines 543-545)

**The movement.** It says the "do nots" are rooted higher than the "dos": what a king hates shows his inner self more than what he wants. Punishment is a kindness that cleans. And the deeper one falls, the higher one can rise. It ends the gate with severity included in kindness, "the foundation of all Torah and mitzvot". The mind is given a sober hope.

> וא"כ עלייתו יותר נעלה כל שירידתו יותר למטה
> *So his rising is higher, the lower his descent.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the 365 prohibitions are rooted in the higher letters of the Name; the 248 positive mitzvot in the lower (line 543). Because they are so hated before Him, they point to what opposes His inner self most, higher than His inner will (line 543). "One knows a king more by what he utterly hates than by his words" (line 543). Step by step: there are harsh severities that destroy, and sweetened severities that repair, like Sheol and Gehinnom, which clean the soul so that it can receive the Shechinah (line 544). So all that harsh punishment is called "a great and true kindness"; "He brings down to Sheol" in judgment that is mercy (line 544). "So his rising is higher, the lower his descent" (line 544). And the order now: severity included in kindness, "to include the left in the right, which is the foundation of all Torah and mitzvot" (line 545). The power of wisdom not to bear a lie is the severity of the Father, "and all is one" (line 545). Bring it home. The things you must not do guard something precious. And if you fell far, the text says the rising can be as high as the fall was deep. The picture of punishment breaks here: this is not a threat. It is the promise that nothing is wasted, even a fall.

**From inside.** Respect for boundaries. A hope that does not deny the fall. Some feel their lowest point become a starting place.

**At the edge.** It points to the prohibitions touching "His inner Essence" by negation (line 543). The mind knows the boundary; what it guards is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Keep one prohibition carefully today, and think: "This guards something You love."

**Ladder.** L12 (Darkness turned to light)

## Shaar HaTefillin §24: "Set me as a seal upon Your heart"; the hidden love that rises (lines 547-548)

**The movement.** It opens the Gate of Tefillin with the seal: Israel ask to be engraved on God's heart, and His tefillin say "who is like Your people Israel". Then it shows the love of God hidden inside the animal soul rising in prayer like a raised letter. The mind is invited to let its hidden love come out.

> וכשמתפעל באהבה האלקית הרי יוצאת מהעלמה ולכך נק' אהבה המוסתרת
> *And when he is moved with Godly love, it comes out of its hiding; this is why it is called the hidden love.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Set me as a seal upon Your heart, as a seal upon Your arm" (line 547). Israel ask God to wear them, and God's own tefillin say "Who is like Your people Israel, one nation on earth" (line 547). A seal makes a mark: raised letters make sunken ones in the wax, and sunken make raised (line 547). Step by step: the animal soul, with its natural will and feelings, hides the Godly light of the divine soul, like a sackcloth garment (line 548). "When he is moved with Godly love, it comes out of its hiding; this is why it is called the hidden love": it was hidden in the animal soul's nature, and it rises into view like a raised letter, like a mountain (line 548). The text's point: the love was not made in prayer; it was there all along, covered by the animal soul's natural wants, and prayer only uncovers it (line 548). The more it was hidden, the more it stands out when it rises, like a mountain (line 548). Bring it home. There is a love for God hidden in you under your ordinary wants. Prayer is where it comes out. You do not need to create it; only let it rise. The picture of the seal breaks: wax takes any seal. This love was already shaped by Him.

**From inside.** A warmth surfacing from under daily concerns. Some feel surprised at how much love was there.

**At the edge.** It points to Israel on "His heart", in His own tefillin (line 547). The mind can feel the love rising; being worn by Him is beyond it.

**Into the day.** In prayer today, pause on one word of love, "ve-ahavta", and let the hidden love come up.

**Ladder.** L01 (Under everything, the spark)

## Shaar HaTefillin §25: "Shema" as gathering; something from nothing; a root in the very Essence (lines 549-551)

**The movement.** It reads "Shema" as gathering the scattered sparks and names the love each Jew inherits. It explains creation from nothing through letters separate from the Creator, so He does not change. And it argues that Israel have a root in the very Essence, because every Jew can give his life, and self-giving is only for the Essence. The mind is shown its own root.

> ואין זה רק מצד שרשם בעצמות ממש
> *And this is only because of their root in the very Essence.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "How good is our inheritance": in every Jew there is a hidden love, rooted in his soul like nature, only needing to be brought out (line 549). "Shema" gathers: the sparks scattered in foreign desires gather and rise (line 549). "Havayah Elokeinu" renews creation every day (line 549). Step by step: creation is from nothing, with "no change at all" in the Creator before and after (line 549). A soul's life in the body does not touch the soul's essence; only more or less light in the body (line 550). So Malchut gives life through "letters", which are "not of its essence at all", like letters separate from the self (line 550). And "with all your might": Israel's root is in thought, yet they can give their lives, and self-giving reaches only the Essence. So "this is only because of their root in the very Essence" (line 551). Everything drawn from the essence is drawn back to its root, "like a spark to the flame" (line 551). Bring it home. If you ever felt that you could give everything for God, even once, the text says that feeling proves where you come from: from His very self. The picture of spark and flame breaks: a spark flies away and goes out. This spark stays rooted.

**From inside.** Dignity. A sense of belonging at the deepest level. Some feel a quiet readiness, without drama.

**At the edge.** Yes. The love of self-giving is "not limited in any vessel", since no thought grasps Him there (line 551). The mind cannot hold the root; it can know it.

**Into the day.** At "with all your might" in the Shema, remember your root and say it as one who belongs.

**Ladder.** L01 (Under everything, the spark)

## Shaar HaTefillin §26: the raised seal and the sunken seal; the whisper of the Amidah (lines 552-556)

**The movement.** It sets the whole of prayer on the image of the seal. Everything up to the end of the Shema is "raised", noisy excitement from below, the bitul of a something. The silent Amidah is "sunken": the inner bitul of the true nothing, like R. Akiva's bowing, and it makes room to receive. The mind moves from excitement to a hollowed quiet.

> והוא בחי' בטול פנימי דעצם אין דחכמה שנק' בטול במציאו'
> *And this is the inner bitul of the essential "nothing" of wisdom, called bitul in existence.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: all the stirring of angels and souls up to the end of the Shema is "raised", with great noise (line 553). It is the bitul of a "something" to nothing, not the nothing itself (line 553). The Amidah is "the silent prayer", "a still, small voice, and there the King comes" (lines 552-553). Step by step: the Amidah is "the inner bitul of the essential 'nothing' of wisdom, called bitul in existence", like the bowing of R. Akiva (line 554). It is like a sunken seal: the letters are hollow, a space to receive (line 554). The eighteen blessings are eighteen channels of light into those hollow vessels (line 555). The rainbow picture: the sun's colors stand out in a clear cloud more than in the sun itself (line 553). So the raised excitement shows more; the essential nothing shows nothing, and receives everything (lines 553-554). The raised seal below is what forms the sunken seal (line 556). Bring it home. In the first part of prayer, let yourself be stirred. At the Amidah, let the stirring go quiet and become a hollow, a place to receive. The picture of wax breaks: wax is passive. This hollow is made by the soul's own deepest yes.

**From inside.** A shift from noise to stillness. Emptiness that feels like readiness. Some feel the Amidah as receiving, not saying.

**At the edge.** Yes. The sunken seal receives "the lights of the Essence of the Emanator" (line 556). The mind can quiet itself; what fills the hollow is beyond it.

**Into the day.** At the start of the Amidah today, take three steps forward and say inwardly: "Now I am a hollow to receive."

## Shaar HaTefillin §27: the arm tefillin; "His left hand is under my head" (lines 557-560)

**The movement.** It reads the arm tefillin as the sunken seal, the bitul of the seated prayer, and the joy of the mother over her children. Then it gives a tender picture: "His left hand under my head": He lifts the head of Israel with His left arm. The mind is held and lifted.

> שמאלו מניח תחת לראשי ומגביהו
> *"His left hand is under my head", and He lifts it.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "as a seal upon your arm" is the arm tefillin, the prayer said sitting, from Yotzer to the end of the Shema (line 557). The four passages in the arm tefillin are all bitul from below, so that it draws from above, "spirit brings spirit" (line 557). Step by step: love and fear are born from understanding, as children from a mother (line 558). The joy of the mother, the root, is greater than the joy of the children (line 559). This is the left arm of the Holy One, where Israel are sealed (line 559). "When He puts on tefillin He puts on the arm first", "and bind them" first (line 560). "His left hand is under my head": by the sunken seal, the inner bitul, He lifts the head of Israel with His left arm, "and lifts it", in the plain meaning of the verse (line 560). Bring it home. When you put on the arm tefillin, or bow your head, picture His hand under your head, lifting you. Your bitul is the place He holds you. The picture of a hand breaks: He has no body. The text uses the verse's own image to say how close the holding is.

**From inside.** Being held. Tenderness in humility. Some feel their bowed head lifted.

**At the edge.** It points to the inner, essential bitul through which He lifts (line 560). The mind can bow; the lifting is His.

**Into the day.** When you bow in prayer today, picture His hand under your head, and rise slowly.

## Shaar HaTefillin §28: known by negation; the silent stone; the deed as the only vessel (lines 561-564)

**The movement.** It says love and longing never grasp the Essence; a king is known more by what he refuses than by what he wants. Only becoming like a silent stone makes one a vessel for Godliness itself, and bowing the physical head is higher than inner bitul. The only vessel for the Essence in the future is the physical deed of the mitzvah. The mind is humbled and grounded.

> אין לו כלי רק דוקא גופה של מעשה המצוה שלמטה בפ"מ
> *It has no vessel except the very body of the mitzvah's physical deed below.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the sunken seal made below makes a raised seal above (line 561). The Essence's greatness over creatures cannot be grasped as any "something", not by great love, not by positive mitzvot; only by bitul of will, like the absence in "sit and do nothing" (line 562). This is knowledge by negation (line 562). Step by step: only becoming like a silent stone makes one "a vessel for the revealing of Godliness itself" (line 561). So in the Amidah, bowing and prostrating: the bitul of the physical body is "higher than the inner bitul in mind and heart" (line 562). Miracles now are light breaking through holes in a screen; in the future it will be revealed "below as it is above, with no change" (line 563). The future world, where His inner will is revealed, "has no vessel except the very body of the mitzvah's physical deed below" (line 564). That is why every Jew has a share, even without intention (line 564). "The reward of a mitzvah is the mitzvah" (line 564). Bring it home. Do not despise the plain physical act of a mitzvah because you did it without feeling. The text says that body of the deed is the only vessel for the Essence. The picture of the stone breaks: a stone is dead. The one who bows like a stone is most alive before the King.

**From inside.** Respect for the body in prayer. A quiet, grounded humility. Some feel relief that the deed counts even when the heart is slow.

**At the edge.** Yes. Love and yearning "do not grasp His essence" (line 562). The mind stops; the body bows; the Essence is revealed in the deed.

**Into the day.** When you bow in the Amidah today, let your body do it fully, as the vessel.

**Ladder.** L14 (The unity in your hand)

## Shaar HaTefillin §29: "Who is like Your people Israel"; the parchment that does not change (lines 565-567)

**The movement.** It explains why God's tefillin speak of Israel: Israel's bitul below makes the raised seal above. Then it gives a quiet picture: the white parchment under the letters, written and erased, never changes, yet carries every letter. The mind is shown the surrounding light that bears all without being touched.

> ומוחק וכותב וחוזר ומוחק כו' וכל זה אין שנוי בגופו של קלף
> *One erases and writes and erases again, and in all this there is no change in the parchment itself.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "from where do we know that the Holy One puts on tefillin? And what is written in them? 'Who is like Your people Israel'" (line 565). Israel's sunken bitul below makes the raised seal of His head tefillin above (line 565). "One nation on earth", as He is One (line 565). Step by step: the parchment around the passages is the surrounding light (line 566). The parchment is white, plain and smooth, with no letters; it is essential, and the letters of ink stuck on it are separate from it (line 566). "One erases and writes and erases again, and in all this there is no change in the parchment itself" (line 566). Yet the parchment bears every letter equally (line 566). So the will of the heart surrounds the feelings, above the letters of reason; love born of reason can stop when the reason stops, but the heart's will does not (line 567). "You crown him with will as with a shield" (line 567). Bring it home. Your thoughts and feelings are written and erased all day. Under them is something that does not change. Rest on the parchment, not only on the letters. The picture of parchment breaks: parchment can tear. The surrounding light it points to cannot.

**From inside.** Steadiness under changing moods. A sense of a clean white ground. Some feel calm return quickly after upset.

**At the edge.** It points to the surrounding light, "above the description of the letters" (line 566). The mind lives in letters; the parchment is beyond them.

**Into the day.** When a mood changes today, say: "The letters change; the parchment does not."

**Ladder.** L04 (Held from inside and all around)

## Shaar HaTefillin §30: "though you go here and there, your image remains with Me" (lines 568-570)

**The movement.** It reads the Zohar: a seal leaves its image even after it moves on. The root of the soul stays bound above even when the person runs here and there after his needs. And His love is a desire of His own, not caused from below. The mind is comforted: its image is kept.

> משום דאע"ג דאזיל הכא והכא דיוקני אשתאר בך
> *Because even though one goes here and there, the image remains with You.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Set me as a seal upon Your heart": "like this seal, because even though one goes here and there, the image remains with You" (line 568). "Surely man walks in an image": the root of the soul stays bound above always (line 568). Step by step: the lower parts of the divine soul run here and there, clothed in the animal soul, busy with bodily needs and business (line 568). But the image stays engraved on His heart, "for love is strong as death" (line 569). After prayer, when the feeling has passed, an impression remains, so that one cannot be separated (line 569). And His love for Israel is "a desire for kindness" of His own: "My desire is in her", "you will be a land of desire" (line 570). It is not caused by arousal from below, for the souls were not yet created; it is an essential bond (line 570). Bring it home. On a scattered day, when you run from task to task, remember: your image stays with Him. You have not left His heart. The picture of the seal breaks: a seal's mark can be rubbed away. This image is engraved on His heart.

**From inside.** Comfort in busy days. A sense of being remembered. Some feel less guilty about how scattered they are.

**At the edge.** Yes. His love is "an essential, simple desire", not caused by anything (line 570). The mind can be scattered; the bond is beyond it.

**Into the day.** In the busiest hour today, say once: "My image stays with You."

## Shaar HaTefillin §31: "spirit brings spirit, just like it" (lines 571-573)

**The movement.** It corrects the seal picture: it is not mechanical, raised making sunken. It is "spirit brings spirit, just like it": bitul below draws from the Essence that does not settle in any vessel or "something". Excitement below draws light clothed in vessels. The mind is shown that what it offers shapes what it receives.

> משום דרוח אייתי רוח ואמשיך רוח רוח כמוהו ממש
> *Because spirit brings spirit, and draws spirit, a spirit truly like it.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the text says its own picture is not exact. If "seal" meant a stamp on wax, raised would make sunken (line 571). But it means rising and drawing down, "because spirit brings spirit, and draws spirit, a spirit truly like it" (line 571). Step by step: bitul below draws "from the Essence of the Infinite light, which does not settle in any vessel or 'something' at all" (line 572). When great love comes clothed in vessels, like kindness in physical abundance, it looks more like a "something"; plain love without a vessel shows less and is more (line 572). The text then raises a question against itself: if spirit brings spirit just like it, sunken below should make sunken above (line 573). It leaves the question open for the next sections. Bring it home. What you bring to prayer shapes what comes back. Bring noise, and light comes clothed. Bring quiet self-giving, and what comes is beyond any shape. The picture of the seal breaks here, and the text itself says so. It is a good lesson in how to hold pictures.

**From inside.** Care about the quality of your prayer, not just its length. A readiness to offer quiet. Some feel responsible in a gentle way.

**At the edge.** Yes. What bitul draws "does not settle in any vessel or 'something'" (line 572). The mind can offer itself; the answer is beyond form.

**Into the day.** Before prayer today, ask yourself: "What spirit am I bringing?" and choose quiet.

## Shaar HaTefillin §32-34: why mitzvot hide now; two loves become one; rising by being included (lines 574-581)

**The movement.** It explains why every mitzvah is now a garment: what draws it is our "something". Then it joins our rising love and His descending love, "and they became truly one". And it separates two kinds of rising: rising by clothing the higher, and rising by being included in it like a child in its mother. The mind learns the second, deeper rising.

> כשעולה שם נכלל ומתאחד בו ונתבטל מהותו שם כאילו אינו במציאות בפ"ע כלל
> *When it rises there, it is included and united with it, and its own being is nullified there, as if it did not exist on its own at all.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "Your Teacher will no longer hide" (line 574). Now every mitzvah is a vessel that clothes and hides the light, because what we bring from below is a "something", and "spirit brings spirit" (line 574). Only bitul draws the Essence as it is (line 574). Step by step: "who remembers the kindness of the fathers... for His name's sake, in love" (line 575). Our running love rises, and the higher love comes down, "ve-ahavta", two lights, rising and descending, "and they became truly one" (line 575). The redemption reaches even the smallest soul (line 575). Two ways of rising: in one, the lower goes up and clothes the outside of the higher, like the worlds rising on Shabbat; it stays itself (lines 577-578). In the other, "when it rises there, it is included and united with it, and its own being is nullified there, as if it did not exist on its own at all", like a baby in its mother, "its head between its knees", eating what she eats (lines 580-581). That inclusion draws entirely new light with no garment (line 581). Bring it home. There is prayer where you remain yourself, lifted a little. And there is prayer where you are simply included in Him, like a child held inside. Allow yourself the second kind, even for a moment. The picture of the womb breaks: a child is born and becomes separate. The inclusion the text speaks of is a return, not a start.

**From inside.** Safety and smallness together. The edges of the self soften. Some feel nourished without doing anything.

**At the edge.** Yes. Inclusion draws "new light from the Essence of the Emanator, not in any vessel or garment at all" (line 581). The mind can let go; what it is included in is beyond it.

**Into the day.** At one moment in prayer today, stop asking and simply be included, like a child held.

## Shaar HaTefillin §35: letters that create at every moment (lines 582-583)

**The movement.** It asks why a letter without parchment around it is unfit, and first explains what a letter is: a coming-out of what was hidden, nothing new in the essence. The letters of "Let there be light" make the light from nothing every moment. The mind is brought to a creation that never stops.

> דאותיות יהי אור מהווה את האור מאין ליש בכל רגע
> *For the letters of "Let there be light" bring light into being from nothing at every moment.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: "every letter that is not surrounded by parchment on all four sides is unfit" (line 582). A letter is called "ot" from "coming": like a person who comes into a house, nothing new in him, only now he is seen (line 582). A letter reveals what was hidden in the essence. Step by step: "the letters of 'Let there be light' bring light into being from nothing at every moment" (line 582). They are the divine letters through which the flow comes, "light and firmament" below (line 583). The letters are vessels that limit, and yet only through them can one level join the next, "so that their coming-into-being is constant, from nothing to something" (line 583). The text raises its own question: a middle should join the higher to the lower, but here the letters themselves seem to create (line 583). It leaves the tension standing. Bring it home. Look at the light in your room. The text says the words "Let there be light" are making it now, this moment, from nothing. The picture of a person entering a house breaks: he was somewhere before. The light was nowhere before the letters spoke it.

**From inside.** Freshness. The world feels newly given. Some feel a quiet gratitude for ordinary light.

**At the edge.** It points to the letters as intermediaries whose source is beyond them (line 583). The mind can see the light; the Speaker is beyond it.

**Into the day.** When you turn on a light today, say: "Let there be light," now.

## Shaar HaTefillin §36: hairs and writing; the higher the source, the more hidden it comes (lines 584-587)

**The movement.** It builds a ladder: engraved letters, letters that spread, speech let out into the air, writing, hairs. Then it gives a rule: the higher the source, the more contracted and hidden the flow comes, by a leap. What comes through hair or writing comes from the innermost Essence. The mind is taught to find the highest in the most hidden.

> דכל שהשפע הולך ממקום הגבוה במדרגה ביותר יותר יבא בצמצו' והעלם בדילוג הערך ביותר
> *For the higher the place the flow comes from, the more it comes in contraction and hiding, by a greater leap.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: there are two kinds of letters: engraved ones, part of the thing itself, like a seal; and written ones, ink on parchment, separate from it (line 584). So in a person: letters of the mind, of feeling, of thought, of speech let out into the air, and writing, each a greater leap from the last (lines 584-585). Step by step: hairs are a middle: they grow from the body without limit, cut and grow back like grass, and yet each is tied to its root; cut, they do not hurt, pulled, they do (line 586). The rule: "the higher the place the flow comes from, the more it comes in contraction and hiding, by a greater leap" (line 586). So "on the contrary, this is the very reason": what comes through the hidden, leaping channels comes "from a high place, from the inner Essence of the Emanator" (line 587). The Torah's root is "the ancient parable" (line 587). Bring it home. When help or insight comes to you in a strange, indirect way, through a chance word, a hard moment, a small thing, do not dismiss it. The text says the most hidden channels may carry the highest source. The picture of hair breaks: hair is outside the body. The flow it pictures comes from the inside of the Essence.

**From inside.** Alertness to small signs. Respect for indirect ways. Some feel the day become more meaningful in its details.

**At the edge.** Yes. The hidden flow comes "from the inner Essence of the Emanator" (line 587). The mind sees the thin thread; the source is beyond it.

**Into the day.** Notice one small, indirect thing today that helped you, and thank Him for it.

## Shaar HaTefillin §37-40: the heart's traits are above the mind; in Him all is one; the black straps (lines 588-600)

**The movement.** It argues that the heart's traits are not born of the mind: a child and an animal flee fire and love what is good by nature, and the mind only lights the way. At the sea no reason could tell Israel from Egypt; only His essential will. And in the Essence, He, His will, His wisdom and His traits are all one. Then it asks why the straps are black. The mind is freed from thinking it must feel only what it understands.

> הוא ורצונו וחכמתו ומדותיו הכל א' בבחי' עצמיות ממש
> *He, His will, His wisdom and His traits are all one, in utter essentiality.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: how does wisdom come from nothing? By a leap, like hairs, from the crown (line 588). The whole of wisdom is like white parchment, bearing every letter (line 590). And the straps hang on the heart, a surrounding light for the heart's traits (line 591). Step by step: the heart's traits are natural from birth: a child and an animal flee fire as much as an adult; they love good food and clothing (line 593). The mind only sees more clearly what is good or bad (line 593). So traits born of contemplation can grow beyond the mind and even resist it later, "for their essence is higher than the mind" (line 594). The mind is "only a passage" (line 596). At the sea, only the essential will could choose Israel "for kindness and good, with no reason at all" (line 596). And in the Essence "He, His will, His wisdom and His traits are all one, in utter essentiality" (line 597). The black straps: a surrounding light clothed in the outside comes black; one shining from its own inside comes white; "black fire on white fire" (lines 599-600). The text admits the question is not yet fully settled (line 600). Bring it home. If your love for God runs ahead of your understanding, do not distrust it. The text says the heart's root is higher than the mind. Let the mind light the way, not hold the love back. The picture of the child fleeing fire breaks: a child's nature is blind. The heart's root above the mind is not blind; it is His will.

**From inside.** Freedom to feel before you fully understand. Respect for simple faith. Some feel the heart and mind stop arguing.

**At the edge.** Yes. In the Essence "all are one", beyond will, wisdom and traits (line 597). The mind separates them; He does not.

**Into the day.** Let one feeling of love for God today stand without explaining it to yourself.

## Shaar HaTefillin §41: standing before one much greater; the Amidah as a taste of the future (lines 601-603)

**The movement.** It matches the seated prayer to the arm tefillin, light hidden in vessels, and the standing Amidah to the head tefillin, the revealing of the Essence. Standing itself is bitul, like standing before one far greater. The Amidah is a taste of the future, when the mitzvah itself will be the Name. The book ends in the middle of this argument.

> כי כל עמידה הוא בחי' בטול בעצם כמו העומד לפני מי שגדול הימנו הרבה
> *For all standing is essential bitul, like one who stands before someone much greater than he.*

**Contemplating it.** Breadth: the seated prayer, from Yotzer Or to the Amidah, is the arm tefillin, "His left hand under my head"; the Amidah is the head tefillin, "and His right hand embraces me" (line 601). The running from below draws down light clothed in vessels (line 601). Step by step: in the Amidah, "whoever makes his voice heard is of small faith": loud feeling belongs to smallness of mind and distance (line 602). Bowing at "Blessed" is the complete bitul of the self (line 602). "For all standing is essential bitul, like one who stands before someone much greater than he" (line 602). That is why only kings may sit in the Temple court (line 602). And "the Amidah is a taste of the future" (line 603). In the future the mitzvah itself will be the Name Havayah; the Name will be revealed without the covering of Elokim (line 603). The book stops here, mid-argument, with no ending. Bring it home. Next time you stand for the Amidah, feel what it is to stand before One much greater. Do not raise your voice. Let the standing itself be your bitul and your taste of what is coming. The picture of standing before a great person breaks: a great person is still a person. Here the Greater is beyond all comparison.

**From inside.** Quiet, upright awe. A stillness in the legs and the heart. Some feel the future touch the present.

**At the edge.** Yes. The Amidah is "the revealing of the Essence of Godliness" (line 601), a taste of the time to come (line 603). The mind can stand; the revealing is beyond it, and the book itself ends without closing it.

**Into the day.** At the Amidah today, stand still with feet together and say nothing aloud, and simply stand before Him.

## 3. The ten deepest contemplations

1. **Introduction (the author's preface), line 6:** כי לא חפץ ה' באלה כ"א בכונה אחת לבד לקשר נפשו אל אמתת עצמותו ית' דוקא. *The book's door: not many intentions, one. To bind your soul to Him Himself, and the unity dwells only where you do not feel yourself.* Before the Shema, drop every clever kavanah and keep one: "to You, Yourself".

2. **Shaar Kriat Shema §8, line 122:** הנה זהו הנק' בשם יחיד כמו היחיד שאינו בגדר מספר. *God as He is: Yachid, alone, the one that is not a number, above even "One".* At "Echad", let "One" open for a moment into "alone", and say "You alone" slowly.

3. **Shaar Kriat Shema §23, line 179:** הוא מבחי' כחו של אא"ס שבתחלה כי הארץ סוף הכל שאין עוד למטה הימנה לכך נמצא בה כח התחלה. *The book's boldest claim: the lowest place, the end of all, holds the power of the very beginning.* Touch the ground or a plain object and say: "The end holds the beginning."

4. **Shaar HaTefillin §31, line 571:** משום דרוח אייתי רוח ואמשיך רוח רוח כמוהו ממש. *The rule under all of prayer: what you bring up shapes what comes down. Excitement draws garments; bitul draws Him.* Before prayer ask "What spirit am I bringing?" and bring quiet self-giving.

5. **Shaar Kriat Shema §24, line 185:** ובכי' זו מגעת לעומק הענג האלקי יותר ממה שהי' עונג אלקי בתחלה. *The soul was sent into a body so that it could weep, and the weeping reaches deeper than the delight it had above.* Let honest tears over distance from God come if they come; not on a crushed day, and stop if it flattens you.

6. **Shaar HaTzitzit §15, line 514:** וזה כח התשוב' אמיתי' שעוש' מן הרע עצמו טוב. *Teshuvah does not only push evil away; it turns the evil itself into good.* Take one thing you regret and turn its energy into one good act today.

7. **Shaar Kriat Shema §50, line 283:** והשוב הוא העיקר וכל ההעלא' דרצוא לא הי' רק בשביל הירידה דשוב. *The running up is for the sake of the return; coming back down into life is the main thing.* After a high moment in prayer, go and do one plain mitzvah at once.

8. **Shaar HaTefillin §28, line 564:** אין לו כלי רק דוקא גופה של מעשה המצוה שלמטה בפ"מ. *Love and yearning do not grasp the Essence; the plain physical deed is the only vessel the future has.* Bow fully in the Amidah and let the body's deed be the vessel.

9. **Shaar Kriat Shema §100, line 455:** כשלומד הל' סוכה ואתרוג מביא וממשיך עצמות אא"ס ברצון העליון הפשוט. *Learning the laws of a mitzvah draws the Essence into its root, because wisdom is "nothing" and only there does the Nothing rest.* Learn one law of the next mitzvah you will do, and say: "I am drawing You into it."

10. **Shaar Kriat Shema §55, line 302:** שזהו עיקר כבודו להיות בהעל' דווקא. *Some secrets are His glory to keep; seeking them hurts the hidden light.* When something in Torah stays closed, say "Your glory is to be hidden", and learn what you can.

## 4. Where this book's way of contemplating differs

*Compared with the Chabad method as mapped in `Chabad Library/hisbonenus/00_HISBONENUS.md`, and with the other books in this index. The differences are kept, not smoothed.*

**One intention, not a map.** The hisbonenus ontology lists several objects of contemplation (the immanent and transcendent light, the order of descent, absolute unity, God's greatness, one's own soul) and treats choosing among them as a practical tool. The Gate of Unity, by the same author, walks the whole chain of worlds rung by rung. Imrei Binah opens by throwing the many out: God "does not want these, only one intention", to bind the soul to His Essence (line 6). The detail that follows is all in service of saying one verse. It is contemplation inside the Shema, tzitzit and tefillin, not a separate sitting with its own object.

**Bitul over arousal, stated as a rule.** The ontology's five stages run through arousal to drawing down, and it records that the Mitteler Rebbe, in Kuntres HaHitpa'alut, names Godly arousal as contemplation's true aim while warning that arousal without a prior "decay" of the self coarsens. Imrei Binah is sharper. Love and excitement from below are a "something", and by "spirit brings spirit" they draw down light clothed in vessels; only bitul, like a silent stone before the King, draws the Essence with no vessel (lines 561, 571-572, 574). R. Aharon of Strashelye's defence of felt arousal stands against this. The index keeps the disagreement and does not pick a side for the reader.

**The heart above the mind.** The ontology says the claim that makes the whole method possible is that the mind rules the heart, and the Tanya (ch. 3) calls wisdom, understanding and knowledge the "mothers" of the traits. Imrei Binah, late in the Gate of Tefillin, argues the reverse at length: the traits are natural from birth, the mind only lights the way and is "only a passage", and traits born of contemplation can outgrow the mind (lines 593-596). The book does not drop contemplation for this; its whole Gate of the Shema is contemplation. It holds both, and so does this reading.

**The body is higher than the inner state.** Most of the method measures a sitting by what happens inside: grasp, feeling, bitul. Imrei Binah says bowing the physical head in the Amidah is higher than the bitul in mind and heart (line 562), that the plain deed is the only vessel of the world to come (line 564), and that "whoever makes his voice heard" in the Amidah shows smallness of mind (line 602). The ontology's last stage, drawing down into a concrete act, agrees in direction; this book goes further and ranks the deed above the state.

**Weeping as a way in.** The ontology's diagnostic layer treats bitterness and sadness as a state to manage: take the resolution if it sharpens, stop if it flattens. Imrei Binah says the soul was sown in the body so that it could weep, and that this weeping "reaches the depth of the divine delight" more than the delight the soul had above (line 185); the penitent's weeping soul is its proof that the servant's bitul reaches the Essence (line 424). The index keeps the manual's guard: not on a crushed day, and stop if it flattens.

**Fear and love ranked, and ranked differently.** The Tanya builds love on fear and gives the great love to one complete in fear. Imrei Binah calls love "the main service" of the Shema (lines 261-262), and then, in a gloss, puts the servant's total bitul above "the most wondrous love" as what reaches the Essence (line 424), while still quoting the Zohar's praise of love. It also splits love and fear by distance: love from far, fear from near, and then the reverse from the receiver's side (lines 367-368). Both are on the page and both stay here.

**Some doors stay shut.** The Gate of Unity asks everyone to contemplate the Essence before the tzimtzum, "there too in detail". Imrei Binah names a secret that will never be revealed and says not to seek it even for oneself, "for this is His main glory: to be hidden" (line 302). Here the Mitteler Rebbe stands nearer the Tanya's reserve than his own other book does.

**Start with the plainest thing, and aim to know.** The Tanya's Shaar HaYichud leaves some things "only to be believed". Imrei Binah says the Shema is where faith above the mind comes into knowing (line 4), and that contemplating how heaven and earth come from nothing is "as if" contemplating the Essence itself (line 191). The Gate of Unity makes the reader earn the whole through long detail; this book lets the plainest act of creation carry the whole.

**Silence and the whisper.** The Piaseczner uses song, scenes and the body; the Rashab's manuals refine traits over time. Imrei Binah's quietest pictures have no parallel in the ontology: the scholar who falls silent until the truth comes, his lips moving without sound (line 389), and the silent Amidah as a sunken seal, a hollow to receive (lines 553-554). It uses the picture of wine and joy (line 299) only as a picture; this reading turns it into song and never into drink.

**Hope for the low place, without softening sin.** The book says the lowest places hold the highest root (lines 179, 497) and that the penitent stands above the perfectly righteous (lines 515, 518). It also says the sin itself is "separated in essence entirely from His unity", worse than Sheol (line 542), while Sheol is never wholly apart from Him (line 541). The text does not reconcile these, and neither does this reading.
